```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/jeevanpillay/Code/@lightfastai/lightfast/thoughts/shared/plans/2026-04-06-internal-trpc-caller-setup.md",
  "content": "# Internal tRPC Caller Setup\n\n## Overview\n\nSet up the internal tRPC infrastructure that enables Inngest functions and `apps/platform` route handlers to call platform capabilities through a typed, middleware-wrapped caller — without JWT overhead. This is the foundation layer; no business logic migration happens here.\n\n## Current State Analysis\n\n`api/platform/src/trpc.ts` defines three procedure tiers (`publicProcedure`, `serviceProcedure`, `adminProcedure`), all using the same observability middleware. Cross-service callers in `packages/platform-trpc/` sign a JWT, construct Headers, and call `platformRouter.createCaller(ctx)` — the full auth ceremony for every call.\n\nInngest functions in `api/platform/src/inngest/functions/` make ~67 async calls (DB, provider APIs, Redis, Inngest dispatch) directly, with no middleware wrapping. Route handlers in `apps/platform/src/app/api/` import lib functions directly from `@api/platform`.\n\n### Key Discoveries:\n\n- `PlatformAuthContext` already has `{ type: \"inngest\" }` and `{ type: \"cron\" }` variants defined but never populated — the auth model anticipated internal callers (`trpc.ts:22-27`)\n- `observabilityMiddleware` is instantiated once at `trpc.ts:104` and reused by all procedure tiers — adding a new tier requires zero middleware changes\n- `createCaller(ctx)` accepts a resolved context object synchronously — no async needed for internal calls (`packages/platform-trpc/src/caller.ts:28`)\n- The `extractAuth` function at `trpc.ts:107-109` only extracts fields for `service` type — needs extension for `internal`\n\n## Desired End State\n\nA `createInternalCaller()` function that returns a fully-typed tRPC caller bound to an `internalRouter`. The caller:\n- Applies the existing observability middleware (structured logging, Sentry tracing, request journaling) to every procedure call\n- Requires zero JWT signing, header construction, or async context creation\n- Is importable from `@api/platform/internal` by both Inngest functions and route handlers\n- Is NOT exposed via HTTP — only accessible through in-process `createCaller()`\n\nVerification: `pnpm build:platform && pnpm --filter @api/platform typecheck` passes.\n\n## What We're NOT Doing\n\n- Migrating any business logic into internal procedures (separate follow-up plan)\n- Changing existing `serviceProcedure` / `platformRouter` / `adminRouter`\n- Adding procedures for DB, provider API, or OAuth operations\n- Modifying Inngest functions or route handlers to use the caller\n- Creating Zod schemas for domain operations\n\n## Implementation Approach\n\nAdd a fourth procedure tier (`internalProcedure`) alongside the existing three, create an empty `internalRouter` with a single health-ping proof procedure, wire up a synchronous caller factory, and export it.\n\n## Phase 1: Add `internalProcedure` and Auth Type\n\n### Overview\n\nExtend the tRPC instance with a new procedure tier for trusted in-process callers. No auth check, full observability.\n\n### Changes Required:\n\n#### 1. Extend `PlatformAuthContext`\n\n**File**: `api/platform/src/trpc.ts`\n**Changes**: Add `internal` variant to the auth discriminated union.\n\n```ts\nexport type PlatformAuthContext =\n  | { type: \"service\"; caller: string }\n  | { type: \"webhook\"; provider: string }\n  | { type: \"internal\"; source: string }\n  | { type: \"inngest\" }\n  | { type: \"cron\" }\n  | { type: \"unauthenticated\" };\n```\n\n#### 2. Update `extractAuth` for Observability\n\n**File**: `api/platform/src/trpc.ts`\n**Changes**: Extend the `extractAuth` callback so internal calls appear in structured logs and Sentry spans.\n\nCurrent (line 107-109):\n```ts\nextractAuth: (ctx) => ({\n  ...(ctx.auth.type === \"service\" && { caller: ctx.auth.caller }),\n}),\n```\n\nNew:\n```ts\nextractAuth: (ctx) => ({\n  ...(ctx.auth.type === \"service\" && { caller: ctx.auth.caller }),\n  ...(ctx.auth.type === \"internal\" && { source: ctx.auth.source }),\n}),\n```\n\n#### 3. Add `internalProcedure` Export\n\n**File**: `api/platform/src/trpc.ts`\n**Changes**: Add after `adminProcedure` (after line 179).\n\n```ts\n/**\n * Internal procedure -- trusted in-process callers only.\n * Used by Inngest functions and platform route handlers via createInternalCaller().\n *\n * NOT exposed over HTTP. Applies observability middleware without auth checks.\n */\nexport const internalProcedure = t.procedure.use(observabilityMiddleware);\n```\n\nNote: This is identical to `publicProcedure` in implementation but distinct in intent. `publicProcedure` is for HTTP endpoints that don't require auth (health checks). `internalProcedure` is for in-process callers that bypass HTTP entirely. Keeping them separate allows diverging middleware later (e.g., adding internal-only tracing tags) without affecting public endpoints.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 2: Create Internal Router and Caller\n\n### Overview\n\nCreate the internal router (empty with a proof-of-concept procedure) and a synchronous caller factory.\n\n### Changes Required:\n\n#### 1. Create Internal Router\n\n**File**: `api/platform/src/internal.ts` (new)\n\n```ts\n/**\n * Internal platform router — in-process callers only.\n *\n * NOT served over HTTP. Accessed exclusively via createInternalCaller().\n * All procedures use internalProcedure (observability middleware, no auth).\n *\n * Sub-routers will be added as business logic is migrated from:\n * - Inngest function steps (DB calls, provider APIs, token management)\n * - Route handler lib calls (OAuth, webhook ingestion)\n */\nimport { createTRPCRouter, internalProcedure } from \"./trpc\";\n\n// -- Internal Router ----------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport const internalRouter = createTRPCRouter({\n  /**\n   * Proof-of-concept procedure.\n   * Validates the full chain: caller -> router -> procedure -> middleware -> response.\n   * Remove once real sub-routers are added.\n   */\n  ping: internalProcedure.query(() => ({\n    ok: true as const,\n    timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),\n  })),\n});\n\nexport type InternalRouter = typeof internalRouter;\n\n// -- Internal Caller ----------------------------------------------------------\n\n/**\n * Create a typed caller for the internal router.\n *\n * No JWT, no headers, no async — just a direct in-process call\n * with full observability middleware on every procedure.\n *\n * Usage in Inngest functions:\n *   const platform = createInternalCaller();\n *   await step.run(\"some-step\", () => platform.someRouter.someProc(input));\n *\n * Usage in route handlers:\n *   const platform = createInternalCaller();\n *   const result = await platform.someRouter.someProc(input);\n */\nexport function createInternalCaller(source = \"unknown\") {\n  return internalRouter.createCaller({\n    auth: { type: \"internal\" as const, source },\n    headers: new Headers(),\n  });\n}\n```\n\n#### 2. Add Package Export Path\n\n**File**: `api/platform/package.json`\n**Changes**: Add `./internal` export alongside existing paths.\n\n```json\n\"./internal\": {\n  \"types\": \"./src/internal.ts\",\n  \"default\": \"./src/internal.ts\"\n}\n```\n\n#### 3. Re-export Types from Index\n\n**File**: `api/platform/src/index.ts`\n**Changes**: Add type export for consumers that need the router type.\n\n```ts\nexport type { InternalRouter } from \"./internal\";\n```\n\nNo runtime exports from index — consumers import `createInternalCaller` from `@api/platform/internal` directly. This keeps the internal router out of the main bundle path.\n\n### Success Criteria:\n\n#### Automated Verification:\n\n- [ ] Type-check passes: `pnpm --filter @api/platform typecheck`\n- [ ] Platform build passes: `pnpm build:platform`\n- [ ] Downstream type-check passes: `pnpm --filter @repo/platform-trpc typecheck`\n\n#### Manual Verification:\n\n- [ ] `internalRouter` is NOT referenced in any `serve()` call or HTTP handler\n- [ ] `createInternalCaller(\"test\").ping()` resolves to `{ ok: true, timestamp: string }` (can verify via a quick test or REPL)\n\n**Implementation Note**: After completing this phase and all automated verification passes, pause here for manual confirmation from the human that the manual testing was successful before proceeding.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 3: Inngest Caller Convenience Export\n\n### Overview\n\nCreate a pre-configured internal caller for Inngest functions so every function doesn't repeat `createInternalCaller(\"inngest\")`.\n\n### Changes Required:\n\n#### 1. Create Inngest-Specific Caller\n\n**File**: `api/platform/src/inngest/platform.ts` (new)\n\n```ts\n/**\n * Pre-configured internal tRPC caller for Inngest functions.\n *\n * Import this in any Inngest function to call platform procedures:\n *\n *   import { platform } from \"../platform\";\n *   await step.run(\"get-token\", () => platform.tokens.resolve(id));\n *\n * Module-level instantiation is safe because:\n * - createInternalCaller() is synchronous (no JWT, no async context)\n * - The caller is stateless — each procedure call creates its own middleware chain\n * - The auth context ({ type: \"internal\", source: \"inngest\" }) is static\n */\nimport { createInternalCaller } from \"../internal\";\n\nexport const platform = createInternalCaller(\"inngest\");\n```\n\n#### 2. Create Route Handler Caller (Optional)\n\n**File**: `apps/platform/src/lib/internal-caller.ts` (new)\n\n```ts\n/**\n * Pre-configured internal tRPC caller for platform route handlers.\n *\n * Usage in route handlers:\n *   import { platform } from \"@/lib/internal-caller\";\n *   const result = await platform.webhooks.persistAndDispatch(input);\n */\nimport { createInternalCaller } from \"@api/platform/internal\";\n\nexport const platform = createInternalCaller(\"route\");\n```\n\n### Success Criteria:\n\n#### Automated Verification:\n\n- [ ] Type-check passes: `pnpm --filter @api/platform typecheck`\n- [ ] Platform app type-check passes: `pnpm --filter lightfast-platform typecheck`\n- [ ] Full check: `pnpm check`\n\n---\n\n## Testing Strategy\n\n### Automated Tests:\n\nThis is infrastructure-only — no business logic to unit test. Verification is that the type system and build pipeline accept the new exports.\n\n### Manual Testing Steps:\n\n1. Import `createInternalCaller` in a scratch file, call `.ping()`, confirm the observability middleware fires (check log output for `[trpc] ok` with `source: \"test\"`)\n2. Verify the internal router is not accessible via HTTP by confirming no `serve()` or `fetchRequestHandler` references it\n\n## Performance Considerations\n\n- `createInternalCaller()` is synchronous — no JWT signing (saves ~1-2ms per call vs `createPlatformCaller()`)\n- The caller itself is cheap to instantiate (just `router.createCaller(ctx)`)\n- The observability middleware adds ~0ms in production (dev mode adds 100-500ms artificial latency, controlled by `opts.isDev`)\n- Module-level instantiation (`export const platform = createInternalCaller(\"inngest\")`) means zero per-call overhead for caller creation\n\n## Architecture Notes\n\n```\nExisting (unchanged):\n  api/app ──JWT──> platformRouter (serviceProcedure)\n  apps/app ──JWT──> platformRouter (serviceProcedure)\n\nNew:\n  Inngest functions ──direct──> internalRouter (internalProcedure)\n  apps/platform routes ──direct──> internalRouter (internalProcedure)\n```\n\nThe two router trees (`platformRouter` and `internalRouter`) are independent. Shared procedure implementations can be composed into both routers in later phases via shared handler functions, but that's out of scope here.\n\n## References\n\n- Research: `thoughts/shared/research/2026-04-05-parseerror-full-propagation-inventory.md` — identified 67 async call sites in Inngest functions\n- Existing tRPC setup: `api/platform/src/trpc.ts` — procedure tiers and middleware\n- Existing caller: `packages/platform-trpc/src/caller.ts` — JWT-based service caller pattern\n- Observability middleware: `vendor/observability/src/trpc.ts` — full middleware implementation\n"
}
```

```
## Analysis: `apps/platform` — Complete Structure and Responsibilities

### Overview

`apps/platform` is a purely server-side Next.js app (port 4112, domain `platform.lightfast.ai`) with no frontend UI beyond a single redirect page. It has exactly **7 API route files** across 6 endpoint groups, all under `src/app/api/` plus one route group `(trpc)`. The app does four categories of work: OAuth connection lifecycle, webhook ingestion, Inngest background job orchestration, and tRPC cross-service calls. There are no React client components, no authentication via Clerk, and no pages other than a redirect.

---

### Entry Points — All Route Handlers

| HTTP Method | Path | File |
|---|---|---|
| `GET` | `/` (page) | `src/app/page.tsx` — redirects to `/api/health` |
| `GET` | `/api/health` | `src/app/api/health/route.ts` |
| `GET,POST,PUT` | `/api/inngest` | `src/app/api/inngest/route.ts` |
| `POST` | `/api/ingest/[provider]` | `src/app/api/ingest/[provider]/route.ts` |
| `GET` | `/api/connect/[provider]/authorize` | `src/app/api/connect/[provider]/authorize/route.ts` |
| `GET` | `/api/connect/[provider]/callback` | `src/app/api/connect/[provider]/callback/route.ts` |
| `GET` | `/api/connect/oauth/poll` | `src/app/api/connect/oauth/poll/route.ts` |
| `GET,POST,OPTIONS` | `/api/trpc/[trpc]` | `src/app/(trpc)/api/trpc/[trpc]/route.ts` |

The tRPC handler lives inside a route group `(trpc)` — this grouping is purely organizational and has no effect on the URL path.

---

### Route Handler Details

#### 1. `/api/health` (`src/app/api/health/route.ts:1-9`)
Static liveness probe. Returns `{ status: "ok", service: "memory", timestamp }`. No dependencies, no DB calls. The `service` field says `"memory"` (a stale label from earlier naming).

#### 2. `/api/inngest` (`src/app/api/inngest/route.ts:1-3`)
Delegates entirely to `createInngestRouteContext()` from `@api/platform/inngest`. Registers all 11 Inngest functions with the Inngest cloud. Exposes `GET` (function discovery), `POST` (event dispatch), and `PUT` (sync). The 11 registered functions are:

1. `ingestDelivery` — webhook delivery → transform → emit `event.capture`
2. `platformEventStore` — fast path: store facts + entities
3. `platformEntityGraph` — entity edge resolution via co-occurrence
4. `platformEntityEmbed` — entity narrative embed to Pinecone
5. `platformNotificationDispatch` — high-significance event notifications via Knock
6. `platformBackfillOrchestrator` — backfill fan-out
7. `platformEntityWorker` — per-entity-type backfill pagination + dispatch
8. `connectionLifecycle` — connection teardown (close gate, revoke, cleanup)
9. `healthCheck` — 5-minute cron: probe all active installations
10. `tokenRefresh` — 5-minute cron: refresh expiring OAuth tokens
11. `deliveryRecovery` — 5-minute cron: sweep stuck webhook deliveries

Defined at `/Users/jeevanpillay/Code/@lightfastai/lightfast/api/platform/src/inngest/index.ts:50-68`.

#### 3. `/api/ingest/[provider]` (`src/app/api/ingest/[provider]/route.ts`) — Webhook Ingestion

The most complex route handler. Full data flow:

1. **Provider guard** (lines 61-76): `getProvider(providerSlug)` checks the slug is known; `hasInboundWebhooks(providerDef)` confirms the provider accepts webhooks. Returns 404 or 400 otherwise.
2. **WebhookDef resolution** (`getWebhookDef`, lines 32-49): Three provider kinds are handled — `isWebhookProvider` (direct webhook), `managed` kind (`providerDef.inbound.webhook`), and `api` kind with `inbound` field.
3. **Header validation** (lines 96-108): Iterates the webhook def's `headersSchema.shape` keys, collects matching request headers, and runs `safeParse`. Returns 400 with Zod issues on failure.
4. **Raw body capture** (line 111): `req.text()` — raw bytes required for HMAC.
5. **Signature verification** (lines 114-138): Calls `getProviderConfigs()` to get the signing secret, uses either `webhookDef.verifySignature` directly or derives one via `deriveVerifySignature(webhookDef.signatureScheme)`. Returns 401 on failure.
6. **Payload parse** (lines 141-163): `JSON.parse(rawBody)` then `webhookDef.parsePayload(jsonPayload)`. Extracts `deliveryId`, `eventType`, `resourceId` via webhook def extractors.
7. **DB persist** (lines 166-177): Inserts into `gatewayWebhookDeliveries` with `onConflictDoNothing()` (idempotent by `deliveryId`).
8. **Inngest dispatch** (lines 180-194): Sends event `"platform/webhook.received"` with dedupe ID `wh-{provider}-{deliveryId}`. Generates a fresh `correlationId` via `crypto.randomUUID()`.
9. Returns `{ status: "accepted", deliveryId }`.

#### 4. `/api/connect/[provider]/authorize` (`src/app/api/connect/[provider]/authorize/route.ts`)
`GET` handler. Reads `org_id`, `connected_by`, and `redirect_to` from query params. Calls `buildAuthorizeUrl()` from `@api/platform/lib/oauth/authorize`. Returns `{ url, state }` JSON. Comments note this is a fallback — the main flow uses tRPC `connections.getAuthorizeUrl`.

#### 5. `/api/connect/[provider]/callback` (`src/app/api/connect/[provider]/callback/route.ts`)
`GET` handler. OAuth provider redirects here after authorization. Delegates entirely to `processOAuthCallback()` from `@api/platform/lib/oauth/callback`. Maps the `CallbackProcessResult` union to three response shapes:
- `"redirect"` → `NextResponse.redirect(result.url)`
- `"inline_html"` → raw HTML response (for popup-window OAuth flows)
- `"error"` → JSON error response

#### 6. `/api/connect/oauth/poll` (`src/app/api/connect/oauth/poll/route.ts`)
`GET` handler. CLI-oriented polling endpoint. Reads `state` token from query params, calls `getOAuthResult(state)` from `@api/platform/lib/oauth/state`. Returns `{ status: "pending" }` if no result yet, or the full result object when complete. The state token itself is the auth mechanism (cryptographically random nanoid).

#### 7. `/api/trpc/[trpc]` (`src/app/(trpc)/api/trpc/[trpc]/route.ts`)
tRPC fetch adapter. The `platformRouter` contains three sub-routers:
- `connections.*`
- `proxy.*`
- `backfill.*`

CORS is applied manually via `setCorsHeaders` — only requests from `appUrl` (resolved via `@vercel/related-projects` to `lightfast-app` in prod or `http://localhost:3024` in dev) receive CORS headers. Preflight `OPTIONS` returns 204 with those headers. `createPlatformTRPCContext` receives `req.headers` for JWT extraction.

---

### Infrastructure Files

#### `src/middleware.ts`
No Clerk — platform uses JWT service tokens. Middleware applies only security headers via `@vendor/security/middleware` (nosecone). Matches all non-static routes plus `/(api|trpc)(.*)`. CORS is not handled here; it is per-endpoint in the tRPC route.

#### `src/instrumentation.ts`
Next.js instrumentation hook. Initializes Sentry for both `nodejs` and `edge` runtimes on startup. Two filters in `beforeSend`:
- Drops `TRPCError` instances with HTTP status < 500 (client errors are not Sentry events)
- Drops `NonRetriableError` from Inngest (expected business rejections)
- Exports `captureRequestError` as `onRequestError` for Next.js request-level error capture.

#### `src/lib/internal-caller.ts`
Creates a pre-configured tRPC caller (`platform`) via `createInternalCaller("route")` from `@api/platform/internal`. This allows route handlers to call tRPC procedures directly without HTTP — currently available but not used by any of the route handlers above (they all call `@api/platform` lib functions directly).

#### `src/lib/related-projects.ts`
Resolves `appUrl` using `@vercel/related-projects` — maps the `"lightfast-app"` project name to the actual URL per environment. Used by the tRPC route handler for CORS origin checking.

#### `src/env.ts`
Assembles the env schema from multiple vendor presets (`vercel`, `sentryEnv`, `betterstackEnv`, `upstashEnv`, `providerEnv`, `inngestEnv`) plus two platform-specific server secrets: `SERVICE_JWT_SECRET` (min 32 chars) and `ENCRYPTION_KEY` (min 44 chars, used for token vault AES encryption).

#### `src/app/layout.tsx` / `src/app/page.tsx`
Minimal shell. `layout.tsx` renders a bare `<html><body>` with no async work. `page.tsx` is a server component that calls `redirect("/api/health")` — the app has no UI.

---

### What `apps/platform` Does (Complete Answer)

Beyond mounting Inngest and the ingest webhook, the platform does:

1. **OAuth connection lifecycle** — three dedicated routes handle initiating OAuth (`/authorize`), receiving the provider redirect (`/callback`), and polling for CLI-initiated flows (`/poll`)
2. **tRPC cross-service API** — a full tRPC HTTP server at `/api/trpc` exposing `connections.*`, `proxy.*`, and `backfill.*` sub-routers, called by `apps/app` over the network with CORS restricted to the related project URL
3. **Sentry + security headers** — instrumentation-level Sentry initialization with custom filtering, and middleware-level security headers (nosecone) on every request
4. **An internal tRPC caller** (`src/lib/internal-caller.ts`) for route-handler-to-procedure calls without HTTP, currently wired but not actively used by the route handlers

It is entirely server-side. There are no client components, no React state, no Clerk auth, and no meaningful page UI — the root page simply redirects to `/api/health`.
```

```
## Analysis: `@api/platform` tRPC — Definition to Consumption

### Overview

`@api/platform` defines the platform service's tRPC router and exports its types, context factory, JWT utilities, and router values. A bridge package `@repo/platform-trpc` wraps those exports into two consumption modes: a direct in-process caller (used exclusively today) and a React/HTTP client (built but never mounted in any layout). All active callers from `apps/app` go through `@repo/platform-trpc/caller`, which signs a service JWT and calls `platformRouter.createCaller(ctx)` in-process — no HTTP request is made.

---

### Layer 1: Router Definition — `@api/platform`

**`api/platform/src/root.ts`**

Defines and exports two tRPC routers:

- `platformRouter` at line 24 — three sub-routers: `connections`, `proxy`, `backfill`
- `adminRouter` at line 38 — currently empty
- `PlatformRouter` type at line 43
- `AdminRouter` type at line 44

**`api/platform/src/internal.ts`**

Defines a separate `internalRouter` at line 15 that is NOT served over HTTP. It has a single `ping` procedure using `internalProcedure` (no auth check). Exports `createInternalCaller()` at line 50, which builds a `PlatformContext` with `auth.type === "internal"` inline (bypasses `createPlatformTRPCContext`) and calls `internalRouter.createCaller(ctx)` synchronously.

**`api/platform/src/trpc.ts`**

Defines the context type and auth model:

- `PlatformContext` interface at line 38: `{ auth: PlatformAuthContext, headers: Headers }`
- `PlatformAuthContext` discriminated union at line 22: six variants — `service`, `webhook`, `internal`, `inngest`, `cron`, `unauthenticated`
- `createPlatformTRPCContext` at line 53: reads the `Authorization: Bearer <token>` header, calls `verifyServiceJWT`, returns `{ auth: { type: "service", caller }, headers }` on success, falls back to `{ auth: { type: "unauthenticated" } }` on failure
- Three procedure tiers: `publicProcedure` (line 139), `serviceProcedure` (line 147, rejects non-`service` auth), `adminProcedure` (line 172, also requires `caller === "admin"`), and `internalProcedure` (line 204, no auth check)
- `observabilityMiddleware` applied to all procedure tiers via `createObservabilityMiddleware`

**`api/platform/src/lib/jwt.ts`**

- `signServiceJWT(caller)` at line 31: creates an HS256 JWT with `iss=caller`, `aud="lightfast-platform"`, `exp=now+60s`, signed with `env.SERVICE_JWT_SECRET`
- `verifyServiceJWT(token)` at line 50: verifies against same secret, extracts `caller` from the `iss` claim

**`api/platform/src/index.ts`** — what the package exports:

| Export | Line | Kind |
|---|---|---|
| `InternalRouter` type | 10 | type re-export from `./internal` |
| `AdminRouter`, `PlatformRouter` types | 11 | type re-exports from `./root` |
| `adminRouter`, `platformRouter` values | 12 | value re-exports from `./root` |
| `PlatformAuthContext`, `PlatformContext` types | 13 | type re-exports from `./trpc` |
| `createPlatformTRPCContext` | 15 | value export from `./trpc` |
| `PlatformRouterInputs`, `PlatformRouterOutputs` | 18–19 | inferred type utilities |
| `AdminRouterInputs`, `AdminRouterOutputs` | 20–21 | inferred type utilities |
| `VerifiedServiceJWT` type | 23 | type re-export from `./lib/jwt` |
| `signServiceJWT`, `verifyServiceJWT` | 26 | value exports from `./lib/jwt` |
| `createCallerFactory` | 28 | value export from `./trpc` |

---

### Layer 2: Bridge Package — `@repo/platform-trpc`

The package's `package.json` exports five subpath entries: `./caller`, `./client`, `./server`, `./react`, `./types`. No `"react-server"` export condition is defined.

**`packages/platform-trpc/src/caller.ts`** — non-React backend caller

Imports from `@api/platform`: `createPlatformTRPCContext`, `platformRouter`, `signServiceJWT`.

`createPlatformCaller(caller = "app")` at line 20, wrapped in React `cache()`:
1. Calls `signServiceJWT(caller)` to produce a 60s HS256 token
2. Constructs a `Headers` object with `x-trpc-source: <caller>-service` and `authorization: Bearer <token>`
3. Calls `createPlatformTRPCContext({ headers })` — runs `verifyServiceJWT` internally, returns `{ auth: { type: "service", caller } }`
4. Returns `platformRouter.createCaller(ctx)` — a typed in-process caller with no HTTP transport

**`packages/platform-trpc/src/server.tsx`** — RSC variant

Imports from `@api/platform`: `PlatformRouter` (type), `createPlatformTRPCContext`, `platformRouter`, `signServiceJWT`.

Exports:

- `createPlatformContext` (line 22, `cache()`-wrapped): reads Next.js `headers()`, adds `x-trpc-source: rsc` and a service JWT with `caller="app"`, then calls `createPlatformTRPCContext`
- `getQueryClient` at line 35: `cache(createQueryClient)`, one QueryClient per RSC request
- `platformTrpc` at line 41: `TRPCOptionsProxy<PlatformRouter>` built via `createTRPCOptionsProxy({ router: platformRouter, ctx: createPlatformContext, queryClient: getQueryClient })`
- `createPlatformCaller(caller = "app")` at line 54: identical JWT-sign + in-process caller logic as `caller.ts`
- `HydrateClient` at line 65: wraps children in `HydrationBoundary` with dehydrated QueryClient state
- `prefetch(queryOptions)` at line 74: calls `queryClient.prefetchInfiniteQuery` or `queryClient.prefetchQuery` depending on the query key type field

**`packages/platform-trpc/src/react.tsx`** — HTTP client (unused in any layout)

Imports `PlatformRouter` type from `@api/platform`.

`PlatformTRPCReactProvider` at line 47: creates an HTTP tRPC client with `httpBatchStreamLink` pointing to `${baseUrl}/api/trpc`. Uses `credentials: "include"`. Accepts an optional `getAuthHeaders()` callback to inject auth headers. Mounts `QueryClientProvider` + `TRPCProvider`.

**`packages/platform-trpc/src/types.ts`**

Imports `PlatformRouter` type from `@api/platform`. Re-exports `RouterOutputs` and `RouterInputs` as `inferRouterOutputs<PlatformRouter>` and `inferRouterInputs<PlatformRouter>`.

---

### Layer 3: HTTP Route Handler — `apps/platform`

**`apps/platform/src/app/(trpc)/api/trpc/[trpc]/route.ts`**

Imports `createPlatformTRPCContext` and `platformRouter` directly from `@api/platform`.

- Sets `runtime = "nodejs"` at line 6
- CORS guard at line 8–25: allows only the origin matching `appUrl` (resolved via `VERCEL_RELATED_PROJECTS`), sets `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, `Allow-Methods`, `Allow-Headers`, `Vary`, `Allow-Credentials`
- `OPTIONS` handler at line 27: returns 204 with CORS headers
- `GET`/`POST` handler at line 32: passes request through `fetchRequestHandler` with `endpoint: "/api/trpc"`, `router: platformRouter`, and context built from `req.headers` via `createPlatformTRPCContext`

This is the HTTP surface — it serves the same `platformRouter` over the wire for any future HTTP consumers.

---

### Layer 4: Active Consumers — `apps/app`

Both consuming files import from `@repo/platform-trpc/caller`:

**`api/app/src/router/org/connections.ts`** — 7 call sites

All use `createPlatformCaller()` (default caller `"app"`):

| Line | Procedure called |
|---|---|
| 48 | `platform.connections.getAuthorizeUrl(...)` |
| 118 | `platform.connections.disconnect(...)` |
| 218 | `platform.proxy.execute(...)` — GitHub App installation validation |
| 316 | `platform.proxy.execute(...)` — `get-repo`, then `get-file-contents` in a loop |
| 462 | `platform.connections.disconnect(...)` — Vercel-specific path |
| 683 | `platform.proxy.execute(...)` — inside `enrichInstallation` via `executeApi` callback |
| 749 | `platform.proxy.execute(...)` — inside `listResources` via `executeApi` callback |

**`apps/app/src/lib/proxy.ts`** — 2 call sites

| Line | Function | Procedure called |
|---|---|---|
| 38 | `proxySearchLogic` | `platform.proxy.execute(...)` — per installation, inside `Promise.all` |
| 267 | `proxyCallLogic` | `platform.proxy.execute(...)` — single call after routing flat params to path/query/body |

---

### Data Flow: `createPlatformCaller` → procedure execution

```
Consumer (api/app or apps/app/lib)
  │
  └─ createPlatformCaller()                     [packages/platform-trpc/src/caller.ts:20]
       │
       ├─ signServiceJWT("app")                 [api/platform/src/lib/jwt.ts:31]
       │    └─ signs HS256 JWT, aud="lightfast-platform", exp=now+60s
       │
       ├─ constructs Headers with Bearer token
       │
       ├─ createPlatformTRPCContext({ headers }) [api/platform/src/trpc.ts:53]
       │    └─ verifyServiceJWT(token)           [api/platform/src/lib/jwt.ts:50]
       │         └─ returns { caller: "app" }
       │    └─ returns { auth: { type: "service", caller: "app" }, headers }
       │
       └─ platformRouter.createCaller(ctx)       [api/platform/src/root.ts:24]
            └─ in-process typed caller
                 ├─ connections.*  → serviceProcedure (requires auth.type === "service")
                 ├─ proxy.*        → serviceProcedure
                 └─ backfill.*     → serviceProcedure
```

No HTTP request is made anywhere in this path. The JWT is signed and immediately verified within the same process.

---
```

Good instinct. Here's what I found:

## Current Consumer Map

**`platformRouter` (external, with JWT auth)** — 11 procedures across 3 sub-routers:
- `api/app/src/router/org/connections.ts` — 7 call sites via `createPlatformCaller()`
- `apps/app/src/lib/proxy.ts` — 2 call sites via `createPlatformCaller()`
- `apps/platform/src/app/(trpc)/api/trpc/[trpc]/route.ts` — HTTP handler

**`internalRouter` (no auth)** — currently just `ping`:
- `apps/platform/src/lib/internal-caller.ts` — creates caller, **not yet used by any route handler**

## `apps/platform` Is Indeed Purely Server-Side

Confirmed. No client components, no Clerk, no UI. Just 7 API route handlers:

| Route | Current Implementation | Could Use Internal tRPC? |
|---|---|---|
| `/api/health` | Static JSON | No (trivial) |
| `/api/inngest` | Mounts 11 Inngest functions | Functions themselves → yes |
| `/api/ingest/[provider]` | Direct DB + `inngest.send()` + webhook verification libs | Yes — all async work |
| `/api/connect/[provider]/authorize` | Calls `buildAuthorizeUrl()` from lib | Yes |
| `/api/connect/[provider]/callback` | Calls `processOAuthCallback()` from lib | Yes |
| `/api/connect/oauth/poll` | Calls `getOAuthResult()` from lib | Yes |
| `/api/trpc/[trpc]` | HTTP adapter for `platformRouter` | N/A (already tRPC) |

## What "Expand to the Whole of apps/platform" Means

Three layers of migration, building on what Phase 1/2 of the existing plan already established:

### Layer A: Route Handlers → Internal tRPC

The 4 non-trivial route handlers (`ingest`, `authorize`, `callback`, `poll`) currently call `@api/platform` lib functions directly. These become thin HTTP adapters that delegate to internal procedures:

```ts
// /api/ingest/[provider]/route.ts — AFTER
export async function POST(req, { params }) {
  const { provider } = await params;
  const rawBody = await req.text();
  const headers = Object.fromEntries(req.headers);
  
  const result = await platform.webhooks.ingest({ provider, rawBody, headers });
  return NextResponse.json(result, { status: 202 });
}
```

New internal procedures needed:
- `webhooks.ingest` — verify + persist + dispatch
- `oauth.buildAuthorizeUrl` — OAuth URL generation
- `oauth.processCallback` — OAuth callback handling
- `oauth.pollResult` — state polling

### Layer B: Inngest Functions → Internal tRPC

The 11 Inngest functions currently make ~67 async calls directly. These become workflow orchestrators calling domain-operation procedures:

New internal procedures needed (~20-25):
- `connections.getActive`, `connections.revokeGate`, `connections.disconnectResources`
- `tokens.resolve`, `tokens.refresh`, `tokens.revoke`
- `providers.fetchEntityPage`, `providers.probeHealth`, `providers.resolveResourceMeta`
- `neural.storeObservation`, `neural.upsertEntities`, `neural.resolveEdges`, `neural.embedEntity`
- `jobs.create`, `jobs.updateStatus`, `jobs.complete`
- `backfill.getHistory`, `backfill.persistRunRecords`, `backfill.replayHeldWebhooks`
- `deliveries.findStuck`, `deliveries.replayBatch`

### Layer C: Existing `serviceProcedure` Routers

The current `connections`, `proxy`, `backfill` routers (11 procedures) could potentially be refactored to call internal procedures too — avoiding duplication where a service procedure and an internal procedure do the same thing (e.g., both need "get active token").

## The Architecture After Full Migration

```
┌─ apps/platform (thin HTTP adapters) ─────────────────────┐
│  /api/ingest/[provider]  → platform.webhooks.ingest()    │
│  /api/connect/.../authorize → platform.oauth.authorize() │
│  /api/connect/.../callback  → platform.oauth.callback()  │
│  /api/connect/oauth/poll    → platform.oauth.poll()      │
│  /api/inngest → mounts Inngest functions                 │
│     └─ each function calls platform.* procedures         │
│  /api/trpc/[trpc] → HTTP adapter (unchanged)             │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─ api/platform (tRPC = source of truth) ──────────────────┐
│  internalRouter (no auth, observability middleware)       │
│    ├─ connections.* (~4 procedures)                       │
│    ├─ tokens.* (~3 procedures)                            │
│    ├─ providers.* (~3 procedures)                         │
│    ├─ neural.* (~4 procedures)                            │
│    ├─ jobs.* (~3 procedures)                              │
│    ├─ backfill.* (~3 procedures)                          │
│    ├─ deliveries.* (~2 procedures)                        │
│    ├─ webhooks.* (~1 procedure)                           │
│    └─ oauth.* (~3 procedures)                             │
│                                                          │
│  platformRouter (JWT auth, for cross-service)            │
│    ├─ connections.* → delegates to internal procedures    │
│    ├─ proxy.*                                             │
│    └─ backfill.*                                          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Everything flows through the internal tRPC layer. Inngest functions, route handlers, and service procedures all call the same internal procedures. One tree. One place to debug.

---

**This is a significant expansion** — from the current 2-phase plan (infrastructure + wiring) to a full architectural migration. Want me to rewrite the plan to cover all three layers? I'd phase it as:

1. **Phase 1** (done): Internal router infrastructure
2. **Phase 2**: Route handlers → internal tRPC (4 routes, ~4 new procedures)
3. **Phase 3**: Inngest functions → internal tRPC (~20 new procedures, 11 function rewrites)
4. **Phase 4**: Service procedures delegate to internal procedures (dedup)

```
1	/**
2	 * POST /api/ingest/:provider
3	 *
4	 * Webhook ingestion endpoint for external provider webhooks.
5	 * Validates HMAC signatures and dispatches to the Inngest pipeline.
6	 *
7	 * NOT tRPC — external providers send raw HTTP with HMAC signatures.
8	 */
9	
10	import { inngest } from "@api/platform/inngest/client";
11	import { getProviderConfigs } from "@api/platform/lib/provider-configs";
12	import { db } from "@db/app/client";
13	import { gatewayWebhookDeliveries } from "@db/app/schema";
14	import type { WebhookDef } from "@repo/app-providers";
15	import {
16	  deriveVerifySignature,
17	  getProvider,
18	  hasInboundWebhooks,
19	  isWebhookProvider,
20	} from "@repo/app-providers";
21	import { parseError } from "@vendor/observability/error/next";
22	import { log } from "@vendor/observability/log/next";
23	import type { NextRequest } from "next/server";
24	
25	export const runtime = "nodejs";
26	
27	// ── Helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
28	
29	/**
30	 * Resolve WebhookDef from any provider kind that supports inbound webhooks.
31	 */
32	function getWebhookDef(
33	  providerDef: NonNullable<ReturnType<typeof getProvider>>
34	): WebhookDef<unknown> | null {
35	  if (isWebhookProvider(providerDef)) {
36	    return providerDef.webhook as WebhookDef<unknown>;
37	  }
38	  if (providerDef.kind === "managed") {
39	    return providerDef.inbound.webhook as WebhookDef<unknown>;
40	  }
41	  if (
42	    providerDef.kind === "api" &&
43	    "inbound" in providerDef &&
44	    providerDef.inbound
45	  ) {
46	    return providerDef.inbound.webhook as WebhookDef<unknown>;
47	  }
48	  return null;
49	}
50	
51	// ── Route Handler ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
52	
53	export async function POST(
54	  req: NextRequest,
55	  { params }: { params: Promise<{ provider: string }> }
56	) {
57	  const { provider: providerSlug } = await params;
58	  const receivedAt = Date.now();
59	
60	  // Step 1: Provider guard
61	  const providerDef = getProvider(providerSlug);
62	  if (!providerDef) {
63	    return Response.json(
64	      { error: "unknown_provider", provider: providerSlug },
65	      { status: 404 }
66	    );
67	  }
68	
69	  if (!hasInboundWebhooks(providerDef)) {
70	    return Response.json(
71	      { error: "not_webhook_provider", provider: providerSlug },
72	      { status: 400 }
73	    );
74	  }
75	
76	  return handleStandardWebhook(req, providerSlug, providerDef, receivedAt);
77	}
78	
79	// ── Standard Webhook Path ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
80	
81	async function handleStandardWebhook(
82	  req: NextRequest,
83	  providerSlug: string,
84	  providerDef: NonNullable<ReturnType<typeof getProvider>>,
85	  receivedAt: number
86	): Promise<Response> {
87	  const webhookDef = getWebhookDef(providerDef);
88	  if (!webhookDef) {
89	    return Response.json(
90	      { error: "no_webhook_def", provider: providerSlug },
91	      { status: 400 }
92	    );
93	  }
94	
95	  // Step 4: Webhook header guard
96	  const headersObj: Record<string, string | undefined> = {};
97	  for (const key of Object.keys(
98	    (webhookDef.headersSchema as { shape: Record<string, unknown> }).shape
99	  )) {
100	    headersObj[key] = req.headers.get(key) ?? undefined;
101	  }
102	  const headersParsed = webhookDef.headersSchema.safeParse(headersObj);
103	  if (!headersParsed.success) {
104	    return Response.json(
105	      { error: "missing_headers", issues: headersParsed.error.issues },
106	      { status: 400 }
107	    );
108	  }
109	
110	  // Step 5: Raw body capture (MUST be req.text() — HMAC needs raw bytes)
111	  const rawBody = await req.text();
112	
113	  // Step 6: Signature verification
114	  const configs = getProviderConfigs();
115	  const providerConfig = configs[providerSlug];
116	  if (!providerConfig) {
117	    log.error("[ingest] provider config not found", {
118	      provider: providerSlug,
119	    });
120	    return Response.json(
121	      { error: "provider_not_configured", provider: providerSlug },
122	      { status: 500 }
123	    );
124	  }
125	
126	  const secret = (webhookDef.extractSecret as (config: unknown) => string)(
127	    providerConfig
128	  );
129	  const verify =
130	    webhookDef.verifySignature ??
131	    deriveVerifySignature(webhookDef.signatureScheme);
132	  const isValid = await verify(rawBody, req.headers, secret);
133	  if (!isValid) {
134	    log.warn("[ingest] signature verification failed", {
135	      provider: providerSlug,
136	    });
137	    return Response.json({ error: "signature_invalid" }, { status: 401 });
138	  }
139	
140	  // Step 7: Payload parse + metadata extraction
141	  let jsonPayload: unknown;
142	  try {
143	    jsonPayload = JSON.parse(rawBody);
144	  } catch {
145	    return Response.json({ error: "invalid_json" }, { status: 400 });
146	  }
147	
148	  let parsedPayload: unknown;
149	  try {
150	    parsedPayload = webhookDef.parsePayload(jsonPayload);
151	  } catch (err) {
152	    log.warn("[ingest] payload schema validation failed", {
153	      provider: providerSlug,
154	      error: parseError(err),
155	    });
156	    return Response.json(
157	      { error: "payload_validation_failed", provider: providerSlug },
158	      { status: 400 }
159	    );
160	  }
161	  const deliveryId = webhookDef.extractDeliveryId(req.headers, parsedPayload);
162	  const eventType = webhookDef.extractEventType(req.headers, parsedPayload);
163	  const resourceId = webhookDef.extractResourceId(parsedPayload);
164	
165	  // Persist to DB
166	  await db
167	    .insert(gatewayWebhookDeliveries)
168	    .values({
169	      provider: providerSlug,
170	      deliveryId,
171	      eventType,
172	      installationId: null,
173	      status: "received",
174	      payload: JSON.stringify(parsedPayload),
175	      receivedAt: new Date(receivedAt).toISOString(),
176	    })
177	    .onConflictDoNothing();
178	
179	  // Dispatch Inngest event
180	  const correlationId = crypto.randomUUID();
181	
182	  await inngest.send({
183	    id: `wh-${providerSlug}-${deliveryId}`,
184	    name: "platform/webhook.received",
185	    data: {
186	      provider: providerSlug,
187	      deliveryId,
188	      eventType,
189	      resourceId,
190	      payload: parsedPayload,
191	      receivedAt,
192	      correlationId,
193	    },
194	  });
195	
196	  log.info("[ingest] webhook received", {
197	    provider: providerSlug,
198	    deliveryId,
199	    eventType,
200	    resourceId,
201	    correlationId,
202	  });
203	
204	  return Response.json({ status: "accepted", deliveryId });
205	}
206	
```

```
1	/**
2	 * Internal platform router — in-process callers only.
3	 *
4	 * NOT served over HTTP. Accessed exclusively via createInternalCaller().
5	 * All procedures use internalProcedure (observability middleware, no auth).
6	 *
7	 * Sub-routers will be added as business logic is migrated from:
8	 * - Inngest function steps (DB calls, provider APIs, token management)
9	 * - Route handler lib calls (OAuth, webhook ingestion)
10	 */
11	import { createTRPCRouter, internalProcedure } from "./trpc";
12	
13	// -- Internal Router ----------------------------------------------------------
14	
15	export const internalRouter = createTRPCRouter({
16	  /**
17	   * Proof-of-concept procedure.
18	   * Validates the full chain: caller -> router -> procedure -> middleware -> response.
19	   * Remove once real sub-routers are added.
20	   */
21	  ping: internalProcedure.query(({ ctx }) => ({
22	    ok: true as const,
23	    timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
24	    source: ctx.auth.type === "internal" ? ctx.auth.source : "unknown",
25	  })),
26	});
27	
28	export type InternalRouter = typeof internalRouter;
29	
30	// -- Internal Caller ----------------------------------------------------------
31	
32	/**
33	 * Create a typed caller for the internal router.
34	 *
35	 * No JWT, no headers, no async — just a direct in-process call
36	 * with full observability middleware on every procedure.
37	 *
38	 * Note: This bypasses createPlatformTRPCContext entirely — context is built
39	 * inline. If you add fields to createPlatformTRPCContext's return type,
40	 * update PlatformContext and this function accordingly.
41	 *
42	 * Usage in Inngest functions:
43	 *   const platform = createInternalCaller();
44	 *   await step.run("some-step", () => platform.someRouter.someProc(input));
45	 *
46	 * Usage in route handlers:
47	 *   const platform = createInternalCaller();
48	 *   const result = await platform.someRouter.someProc(input);
49	 */
50	export function createInternalCaller(source = "unknown") {
51	  return internalRouter.createCaller({
52	    auth: { type: "internal" as const, source },
53	    headers: new Headers(),
54	  });
55	}
56
```

```
1	/**
2	 * Platform service tRPC initialization.
3	 *
4	 * Auth model: service-to-service JWT (not Clerk).
5	 * All callers are internal services (app, platform, inngest, cron).
6	 */
7	import { initTRPC, TRPCError } from "@trpc/server";
8	import { parseError } from "@vendor/observability/error/next";
9	import { log } from "@vendor/observability/log/next";
10	import { createObservabilityMiddleware } from "@vendor/observability/trpc";
11	import superjson from "superjson";
12	import { ZodError } from "zod";
13	
14	import { verifyServiceJWT } from "./lib/jwt";
15	
16	// -- Auth Context -------------------------------------------------------------
17	
18	/**
19	 * Discriminated union for platform service authentication.
20	 * Every request resolves to exactly one variant.
21	 */
22	export type PlatformAuthContext =
23	  | { type: "service"; caller: string }
24	  | { type: "webhook"; provider: string }
25	  | { type: "internal"; source: string }
26	  | { type: "inngest" }
27	  | { type: "cron" }
28	  | { type: "unauthenticated" };
29	
30	/** Explicit context type for tRPC initialization.
31	 *
32	 * Must be used instead of `typeof createPlatformTRPCContext` because the
33	 * context factory only returns "service" | "unauthenticated" — but in-process
34	 * callers (createInternalCaller) provide other auth variants directly.
35	 *
36	 * If you add fields to createPlatformTRPCContext's return type, add them here too.
37	 */
38	export interface PlatformContext {
39	  auth: PlatformAuthContext;
40	  headers: Headers;
41	}
42	
43	// -- Context Creation ---------------------------------------------------------
44	
45	/**
46	 * Create tRPC context for platform service requests.
47	 *
48	 * Auth resolution order:
49	 * 1. Bearer JWT in Authorization header -> service auth
50	 * 2. x-trpc-source header for internal identification
51	 * 3. Unauthenticated fallback
52	 */
53	export const createPlatformTRPCContext = async (opts: {
54	  headers: Headers;
55	}): Promise<PlatformContext> => {
56	  const source = opts.headers.get("x-trpc-source") ?? "unknown";
57	
58	  // Check for service JWT in Authorization header
59	  const authHeader = opts.headers.get("authorization");
60	  if (authHeader?.startsWith("Bearer ")) {
61	    const token = authHeader.replace("Bearer ", "");
62	
63	    try {
64	      const verified = await verifyServiceJWT(token);
65	      log.info("[trpc] platform service request", {
66	        source,
67	        auth: "service",
68	        caller: verified.caller,
69	      });
70	      return {
71	        auth: {
72	          type: "service" as const,
73	          caller: verified.caller,
74	        },
75	        headers: opts.headers,
76	      };
77	    } catch (error) {
78	      log.warn("[trpc] JWT verification error", {
79	        source,
80	        error: parseError(error),
81	      });
82	    }
83	  }
84	
85	  // No authentication
86	  log.info("[trpc] platform service request", {
87	    source,
88	    auth: "unauthenticated",
89	  });
90	  return {
91	    auth: { type: "unauthenticated" as const },
92	    headers: opts.headers,
93	  };
94	};
95	
96	// -- tRPC Initialization ------------------------------------------------------
97	
98	const isProduction = process.env.NODE_ENV === "production";
99	
100	const t = initTRPC.context<PlatformContext>().create({
101	  transformer: superjson,
102	  errorFormatter: ({ shape, error }) => {
103	    const shouldSanitize =
104	      isProduction && error.code === "INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR";
105	
106	    return {
107	      ...shape,
108	      message: shouldSanitize ? "An unexpected error occurred" : shape.message,
109	      data: {
110	        ...shape.data,
111	        zodError:
112	          error.cause instanceof ZodError ? error.cause.flatten() : null,
113	      },
114	    };
115	  },
116	});
117	
118	// -- Middleware ----------------------------------------------------------------
119	
120	const observabilityMiddleware = t.middleware(
121	  createObservabilityMiddleware({
122	    isDev: t._config.isDev,
123	    extractAuth: (ctx) => ({
124	      ...(ctx.auth.type === "service" && { caller: ctx.auth.caller }),
125	      ...(ctx.auth.type === "internal" && { source: ctx.auth.source }),
126	    }),
127	  })
128	);
129	
130	// -- Router & Procedure Exports -----------------------------------------------
131	
132	export const createTRPCRouter = t.router;
133	export const createCallerFactory = t.createCallerFactory;
134	
135	/**
136	 * Public procedure -- no auth required.
137	 * Use for health checks or publicly accessible endpoints.
138	 */
139	export const publicProcedure = t.procedure.use(observabilityMiddleware);
140	
141	/**
142	 * Service procedure -- requires valid service JWT.
143	 * Used by app or other internal services calling platform.
144	 *
145	 * Guarantees `ctx.auth.type === "service"` and `ctx.auth.caller` is available.
146	 */
147	export const serviceProcedure = t.procedure
148	  .use(observabilityMiddleware)
149	  .use(({ ctx, next }) => {
150	    if (ctx.auth.type !== "service") {
151	      throw new TRPCError({
152	        code: "UNAUTHORIZED",
153	        message:
154	          "Service authentication required. Provide a valid service JWT in the Authorization header.",
155	      });
156	    }
157	
158	    return next({
159	      ctx: {
160	        ...ctx,
161	        auth: ctx.auth as Extract<PlatformAuthContext, { type: "service" }>,
162	      },
163	    });
164	  });
165	
166	/**
167	 * Admin procedure -- requires service JWT from an admin caller.
168	 * Used for administrative operations (reindex, purge, etc.).
169	 *
170	 * Restricts `ctx.auth.caller` to "admin" only.
171	 */
172	export const adminProcedure = t.procedure
173	  .use(observabilityMiddleware)
174	  .use(({ ctx, next }) => {
175	    if (ctx.auth.type !== "service") {
176	      throw new TRPCError({
177	        code: "UNAUTHORIZED",
178	        message: "Service authentication required.",
179	      });
180	    }
181	
182	    if (ctx.auth.caller !== "admin") {
183	      throw new TRPCError({
184	        code: "FORBIDDEN",
185	        message:
186	          "Admin access required. This endpoint is restricted to admin callers.",
187	      });
188	    }
189	
190	    return next({
191	      ctx: {
192	        ...ctx,
193	        auth: ctx.auth as Extract<PlatformAuthContext, { type: "service" }>,
194	      },
195	    });
196	  });
197	
198	/**
199	 * Internal procedure -- trusted in-process callers only.
200	 * Used by Inngest functions and platform route handlers via createInternalCaller().
201	 *
202	 * NOT exposed over HTTP. Applies observability middleware without auth checks.
203	 */
204	export const internalProcedure = t.procedure.use(observabilityMiddleware);
205	
```

```
1	/**
2	 * GET /api/connect/:provider/authorize
3	 *
4	 * Initiate OAuth flow. Ported from apps/gateway/src/routes/connections.ts (lines 79-141).
5	 *
6	 * NOT tRPC — returns authorize URL + state for browser OAuth.
7	 * The main flow uses tRPC `connections.getAuthorizeUrl` instead;
8	 * this route supports direct browser navigation as a fallback.
9	 */
10	
11	import { buildAuthorizeUrl } from "@api/platform/lib/oauth/authorize";
12	import type { SourceType } from "@repo/app-providers";
13	import { log } from "@vendor/observability/log/next";
14	import type { NextRequest } from "next/server";
15	
16	export const runtime = "nodejs";
17	
18	export async function GET(
19	  req: NextRequest,
20	  { params }: { params: Promise<{ provider: string }> }
21	) {
22	  const { provider } = await params;
23	  const providerName = provider as SourceType;
24	
25	  const orgId = req.nextUrl.searchParams.get("org_id");
26	  const connectedBy =
27	    req.nextUrl.searchParams.get("connected_by") ??
28	    req.headers.get("X-User-Id") ??
29	    "unknown";
30	  const redirectTo = req.nextUrl.searchParams.get("redirect_to") ?? undefined;
31	
32	  if (!orgId) {
33	    log.warn("[oauth/authorize] missing org_id", { provider: providerName });
34	    return Response.json({ error: "missing_org_id" }, { status: 400 });
35	  }
36	
37	  const result = await buildAuthorizeUrl({
38	    provider: providerName,
39	    orgId,
40	    connectedBy,
41	    redirectTo,
42	  });
43	
44	  if (!result.ok) {
45	    log.warn("[oauth/authorize] failed to build authorize URL", {
46	      provider: providerName,
47	      error: result.error,
48	    });
49	    return Response.json({ error: result.error }, { status: 400 });
50	  }
51	
52	  log.info("[oauth/authorize] authorize URL built", { provider: providerName });
53	  return Response.json({ url: result.url, state: result.state });
54	}
55
```

```
1	/**
2	 * GET /api/connect/:provider/callback
3	 *
4	 * OAuth callback. Ported from apps/gateway/src/routes/connections.ts (lines 208-489).
5	 *
6	 * NOT tRPC — OAuth provider redirects here directly (browser).
7	 * Maps CallbackProcessResult from the lib layer to HTTP responses.
8	 */
9	
10	import {
11	  type CallbackProcessResult,
12	  processOAuthCallback,
13	} from "@api/platform/lib/oauth/callback";
14	import type { SourceType } from "@repo/app-providers";
15	import { log } from "@vendor/observability/log/next";
16	import { type NextRequest, NextResponse } from "next/server";
17	
18	export const runtime = "nodejs";
19	
20	export async function GET(
21	  req: NextRequest,
22	  { params }: { params: Promise<{ provider: string }> }
23	) {
24	  const { provider } = await params;
25	  const providerName = provider as SourceType;
26	
27	  // Build query dict from all URL search params (null-prototype to prevent prototype pollution)
28	  const query: Record<string, string> = Object.assign(
29	    Object.create(null) as Record<string, string>,
30	    Object.fromEntries(req.nextUrl.searchParams)
31	  );
32	
33	  const state = query.state ?? "";
34	
35	  const result: CallbackProcessResult = await processOAuthCallback({
36	    provider: providerName,
37	    state,
38	    query,
39	  });
40	
41	  switch (result.kind) {
42	    case "redirect":
43	      log.info("[oauth/callback] redirecting", { provider: providerName });
44	      return NextResponse.redirect(result.url);
45	
46	    case "inline_html":
47	      log.info("[oauth/callback] inline html response", {
48	        provider: providerName,
49	        status: result.status ?? 200,
50	      });
51	      return new Response(result.html, {
52	        status: result.status ?? 200,
53	        headers: { "Content-Type": "text/html" },
54	      });
55	
56	    case "error":
57	      log.warn("[oauth/callback] error result", {
58	        provider: providerName,
59	        error: result.error,
60	        status: result.status,
61	      });
62	      return Response.json({ error: result.error }, { status: result.status });
63	
64	    default: {
65	      const _exhaustive: never = result;
66	      return _exhaustive;
67	    }
68	  }
69	}
70
```

```
15	export const runtime = "nodejs";

17	export async function GET(req: NextRequest) {
18	  const state = req.nextUrl.searchParams.get("state");
19	
20	  if (!state) {
21	    log.warn("[oauth/poll] missing state token");
22	    return Response.json({ error: "missing_state" }, { status: 400 });
23	  }
24	
25	  const result = await getOAuthResult(state);
26	
27	  if (!result) {

29	  }
30	
31	  log.info("[oauth/poll] result found", {
32	    state: `${state.slice(0, 8)}...`,
33	  });
34	  return Response.json(result);
35	}
```

```
1	/**
2	 * Build OAuth authorize URL for a provider.
3	 *
4	 * Ported from apps/gateway/src/routes/connections.ts (lines 79-141).
5	 * Returns a data structure — no HTTP responses.
6	 */
7	import type { SourceType } from "@repo/app-providers";
8	import { getProvider } from "@repo/app-providers";
9	import { nanoid } from "@repo/lib";
10	import { log } from "@vendor/observability/log/next";
11	import { providerConfigs } from "../provider-configs";
12	import { storeOAuthState } from "./state";
13	
14	// ── App URL ──
15	
16	/**
17	 * App URL for redirects.
18	 * Used to validate redirect_to parameters.
19	 */
20	const appUrl = (() => {
21	  const vercelEnv = process.env.VERCEL_ENV;
22	  const vercelUrl = process.env.VERCEL_URL;
23	  const productionUrl = process.env.VERCEL_PROJECT_PRODUCTION_URL;
24	
25	  if (vercelEnv === "preview" && vercelUrl) {
26	    return `https://${vercelUrl}`;
27	  }
28	
29	  if (productionUrl) {
30	    return `https://${productionUrl}`;
31	  }
32	
33	  return "http://localhost:3024";
34	})();
35	
36	// ── Types ──
37	
38	interface AuthorizeParams {
39	  connectedBy: string;
40	  orgId: string;
41	  provider: SourceType;
42	  redirectTo?: string;
43	}
44	
45	type AuthorizeResult =
46	  | { ok: true; url: string; state: string }
47	  | { ok: false; error: string };
48	
49	// ── Build Authorize URL ──
50	
51	/**
52	 * Build an OAuth authorize URL for the given provider.
53	 *
54	 * Generates a cryptographically random state token, stores it in Redis,
55	 * and returns the authorization URL for the provider.
56	 *
57	 * @returns AuthorizeResult — either `{ ok: true, url, state }` or `{ ok: false, error }`.
58	 */
59	export async function buildAuthorizeUrl(
60	  params: AuthorizeParams
61	): Promise<AuthorizeResult> {
62	  const { provider, orgId, connectedBy, redirectTo } = params;
63	
64	  const config = providerConfigs[provider];
65	
66	  if (!config) {
67	    log.warn("[oauth/authorize] provider not configured", { provider });
68	    return { ok: false, error: "unknown_provider" };
69	  }
70	
71	  // Validate redirect_to — allowlist: "inline", localhost, or appUrl
72	  if (redirectTo && redirectTo !== "inline") {
73	    try {
74	      const url = new URL(redirectTo);
75	      if (url.hostname !== "localhost" && !redirectTo.startsWith(appUrl)) {
76	        log.warn("[oauth/authorize] invalid redirect_to (not on allowlist)", {
77	          provider,
78	          redirectTo,
79	        });
80	        return { ok: false, error: "invalid_redirect_to" };
81	      }
82	    } catch {
83	      log.warn("[oauth/authorize] invalid redirect_to (malformed URL)", {
84	        provider,
85	        redirectTo,
86	      });
87	      return { ok: false, error: "invalid_redirect_to" };
88	    }
89	  }
90	
91	  const state = nanoid();
92	
93	  // Store OAuth state in Redis (10-minute TTL) — atomic pipeline
94	  await storeOAuthState(state, {
95	    provider,
96	    orgId,
97	    connectedBy,
98	    ...(redirectTo ? { redirectTo } : {}),
99	    createdAt: Date.now().toString(),
100	  });
101	
102	  const providerDef = getProvider(provider);
103	  const auth = providerDef.auth;
104	
105	  let url: string;
106	  if (auth.kind === "oauth") {
107	    // SAFETY: config is providerConfigs[providerName], created by the same provider's
108	    // createConfig(). Runtime type matches TConfig; the service cannot know TConfig
109	    // statically because it serves all providers from a single Record<string, unknown>.
110	    url = auth.buildAuthUrl(config as never, state);
111	  } else if (auth.kind === "app-token") {
112	    url = auth.buildInstallUrl(config as never, state);
113	  } else {
114	    log.warn("[oauth/authorize] provider does not support OAuth", { provider });
115	    return { ok: false, error: "provider_does_not_support_oauth" };
116	  }
117	
118	  return { ok: true, url, state };
119	}
120
```

```
1	/**
2	 * Redis atomic state management for OAuth flows.
3	 *
4	 * Ported from apps/gateway/src/routes/connections.ts (lines 113-168, 180-196).
5	 * Functions return data structures — no HTTP responses.
6	 */
7	import { log } from "@vendor/observability/log/next";
8	import { redis } from "@vendor/upstash";
9	import { oauthResultKey, oauthStateKey } from "../cache";
10	
11	// ── Store OAuth State ──
12	
13	export interface OAuthStateData {
14	  connectedBy: string;
15	  createdAt: string;
16	  orgId: string;
17	  provider: string;
18	  redirectTo?: string;
19	}
20	
21	/**
22	 * Store OAuth state in Redis with a 10-minute TTL.
23	 * Uses an atomic pipeline (HSET + EXPIRE) to prevent partial writes.
24	 */
25	export async function storeOAuthState(
26	  state: string,
27	  data: OAuthStateData
28	): Promise<void> {
29	  const key = oauthStateKey(state);
30	  await redis
31	    .pipeline()
32	    .hset(key, {
33	      provider: data.provider,
34	      orgId: data.orgId,
35	      connectedBy: data.connectedBy,
36	      ...(data.redirectTo ? { redirectTo: data.redirectTo } : {}),
37	      createdAt: data.createdAt,
38	    })
39	    .expire(key, 600)
40	    .exec();
41	
42	  log.info("[oauth/state] state stored", {
43	    provider: data.provider,
44	    orgId: data.orgId,
45	    statePrefix: state.slice(0, 8),
46	  });
47	}
48	
49	// ── Consume OAuth State ──
50	
51	/**
52	 * Atomic read-and-delete to prevent state replay via concurrent requests.
53	 * Returns stateData if valid, null if missing or expired.
54	 */
55	export async function consumeOAuthState(
56	  state: string
57	): Promise<Record<string, string> | null> {
58	  const key = oauthStateKey(state);
59	  const [stateData] = await redis
60	    .multi()
61	    .hgetall<Record<string, string>>(key)
62	    .del(key)
63	    .exec<[Record<string, string> | null, number]>();
64	
65	  if (!stateData?.orgId) {
66	    log.warn("[oauth/state] state not found or expired", {
67	      statePrefix: state.slice(0, 8),
68	    });
69	    return null;
70	  }
71	
72	  log.info("[oauth/state] state consumed", {
73	    statePrefix: state.slice(0, 8),
74	    orgId: stateData.orgId,
75	  });
76	
77	  return stateData;
78	}
79	
80	// ── Store OAuth Result ──
81	
82	export interface OAuthResultData {
83	  error?: string;
84	  provider?: string;
85	  reactivated?: string;
86	  setupAction?: string;
87	  status: "completed" | "failed";
88	}
89	
90	/**
91	 * Store OAuth completion/failure result in Redis for CLI polling (5-min TTL).
92	 */
93	export async function storeOAuthResult(
94	  state: string,
95	  data: OAuthResultData
96	): Promise<void> {
97	  const key = oauthResultKey(state);
98	  const fields: Record<string, string> = { status: data.status };
99	
100	  if (data.provider) {
101	    fields.provider = data.provider;
102	  }
103	  if (data.reactivated) {
104	    fields.reactivated = data.reactivated;
105	  }
106	  if (data.setupAction) {
107	    fields.setupAction = data.setupAction;
108	  }
109	  if (data.error) {
110	    fields.error = data.error;
111	  }
112	
113	  await redis.pipeline().hset(key, fields).expire(key, 300).exec();
114	}
115	
116	// ── Get OAuth Result ──
117	
118	/**
119	 * Poll for OAuth completion. Returns the result hash if present, null if pending.
120	 * Used by CLI to detect when the browser OAuth flow has completed.
121	 */
122	export async function getOAuthResult(
123	  state: string
124	): Promise<Record<string, string> | null> {
125	  return redis.hgetall<Record<string, string>>(oauthResultKey(state));
126	}
127
```

```
1	/**
2	 * Process OAuth callback — the heavyweight OAuth completion logic.
3	 *
4	 * Ported from apps/gateway/src/routes/connections.ts (lines 208-489).
5	 * Returns structured CallbackProcessResult instead of HTTP responses.
6	 * The Route Handler maps CallbackProcessResult to NextResponse.redirect / HTML.
7	 */
8	import { db } from "@db/app/client";
9	import { gatewayInstallations } from "@db/app/schema";
10	import type { SourceType } from "@repo/app-providers";
11	import { getProvider, providerAccountInfoSchema } from "@repo/app-providers";
12	import { and, eq } from "@vendor/db";
13	import { log } from "@vendor/observability/log/next";
14	import { providerConfigs } from "../provider-configs";
15	import { writeTokenRecord } from "../token-store";
16	import { consumeOAuthState, storeOAuthResult } from "./state";
17	
18	// ── App URL ──
19	
20	/**
21	 * App URL for redirects.
22	 */
23	const appUrl = (() => {
24	  const vercelEnv = process.env.VERCEL_ENV;
25	  const vercelUrl = process.env.VERCEL_URL;
26	  const productionUrl = process.env.VERCEL_PROJECT_PRODUCTION_URL;
27	
28	  if (vercelEnv === "preview" && vercelUrl) {
29	    return `https://${vercelUrl}`;
30	  }
31	
32	  if (productionUrl) {
33	    return `https://${productionUrl}`;
34	  }
35	
36	  return "http://localhost:3024";
37	})();
38	
39	// ── Result Types ──
40	
41	/**
42	 * Structured result from processing an OAuth callback.
43	 * The Route Handler maps this to NextResponse.redirect() or HTML response.
44	 */
45	export type CallbackProcessResult =
46	  | { kind: "redirect"; url: string }
47	  | { kind: "inline_html"; html: string; status?: number }
48	  | { kind: "error"; error: string; status: number };
49	
50	// ── Inline HTML Templates ──
51	
52	function successHtml(providerName: string): string {
53	  return `<!doctype html>
54	<html>
55	  <head><title>Connected</title></head>
56	  <body style="font-family:system-ui;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;height:100vh;margin:0;background:#fafafa">
57	    <div style="text-align:center">
58	      <div style="font-size:48px;margin-bottom:16px">&#10003;</div>
59	      <h1 style="margin:0 0 8px">Connected to ${providerName}!</h1>
60	      <p style="color:#666">You can close this tab and return to your terminal.</p>
61	    </div>
62	    <script>setTimeout(()=>window.close(),2000)</script>
63	  </body>
64	</html>`;
65	}
66	
67	function errorHtml(message: string): string {
68	  return `<!doctype html>
69	<html>
70	  <head><title>Connection Failed</title></head>
71	  <body style="font-family:system-ui;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;height:100vh;margin:0;background:#fafafa">
72	    <div style="text-align:center">
73	      <div style="font-size:48px;margin-bottom:16px">&#10007;</div>
74	      <h1 style="margin:0 0 8px">Connection Failed</h1>
75	      <p style="color:#666">${message}</p>
76	      <p style="color:#999;font-size:14px">Close this tab and try again in your terminal.</p>
77	    </div>
78	  </body>
79	</html>`;
80	}
81	
82	// ── Redirect Helpers ──
83	
84	function buildRedirectForCompletion(
85	  stateData: Record<string, string>,
86	  providerName: SourceType,
87	  opts: { reactivated?: boolean; setupAction?: string }
88	): CallbackProcessResult {
89	  const redirectTo = stateData.redirectTo;
90	
91	  if (redirectTo === "inline") {
92	    return { kind: "inline_html", html: successHtml(providerName) };
93	  }
94	
95	  if (redirectTo) {
96	    const redirectUrl = new URL(redirectTo);
97	    if (opts.reactivated) {
98	      redirectUrl.searchParams.set("reactivated", "true");
99	    }
100	    if (opts.setupAction) {
101	      redirectUrl.searchParams.set("setup_action", opts.setupAction);
102	    }
103	    return { kind: "redirect", url: redirectUrl.toString() };
104	  }
105	
106	  // Default: redirect to app (existing behavior, backwards compatible)
107	  const redirectUrl = new URL(`${appUrl}/provider/${providerName}/connected`);
108	  if (opts.reactivated) {
109	    redirectUrl.searchParams.set("reactivated", "true");
110	  }
111	  if (opts.setupAction) {
112	    redirectUrl.searchParams.set("setup_action", opts.setupAction);
113	  }
114	  return { kind: "redirect", url: redirectUrl.toString() };
115	}
116	
117	function buildRedirectForError(
118	  stateData: Record<string, string>,
119	  providerName: SourceType,
120	  message: string
121	): CallbackProcessResult {
122	  const redirectTo = stateData.redirectTo;
123	
124	  if (redirectTo === "inline") {
125	    return { kind: "inline_html", html: errorHtml(message), status: 400 };
126	  }
127	
128	  if (redirectTo) {
129	    return {
130	      kind: "redirect",
131	      url: `${redirectTo}?error=${encodeURIComponent(message)}`,
132	    };
133	  }
134	
135	  return {
136	    kind: "redirect",
137	    url: `${appUrl}/provider/${providerName}/connected?error=${encodeURIComponent(message)}`,
138	  };
139	}
140	
141	// ── Main Callback Processing ──
142	
143	export interface ProcessCallbackParams {
144	  provider: SourceType;
145	  /** All query params from the callback URL */
146	  query: Record<string, string>;
147	  /** The state token from the callback query string */
148	  state: string;
149	}
150	
151	/**
152	 * Process an OAuth callback: validate state, exchange code, upsert installation,
153	 * persist tokens, and return a structured result for the Route Handler to act on.
154	 */
155	export async function processOAuthCallback(
156	  params: ProcessCallbackParams
157	): Promise<CallbackProcessResult> {
158	  const { provider: providerName, state, query } = params;
159	
160	  const providerDef = getProvider(providerName);
161	  const config = providerConfigs[providerName];
162	
163	  if (!config) {
164	    log.warn("[oauth/callback] provider not configured", {
165	      provider: providerName,
166	    });
167	    return {
168	      kind: "error",
169	      error: "unknown_provider",
170	      status: 400,
171	    };
172	  }
173	
174	  let stateData = await consumeOAuthState(state);
175	
176	  // GitHub-initiated redirects (permission changes, reinstalls) arrive without
177	  // our state token. If state is missing but installation_id is present, look up
178	  // the existing installation to recover orgId/connectedBy.
179	  if (!stateData && providerName === "github") {
180	    const installationId = query.installation_id;
181	    if (installationId) {
182	      const existing = await db
183	        .select({
184	          orgId: gatewayInstallations.orgId,
185	          connectedBy: gatewayInstallations.connectedBy,
186	        })
187	        .from(gatewayInstallations)
188	        .where(
189	          and(
190	            eq(gatewayInstallations.provider, "github"),
191	            eq(gatewayInstallations.externalId, installationId)
192	          )
193	        )
194	        .limit(1);
195	
196	      const row = existing[0];
197	      if (row) {
198	        stateData = {
199	          provider: "github",
200	          orgId: row.orgId,
201	          connectedBy: row.connectedBy,
202	        };
203	      }
204	    }
205	  }
206	
207	  if (!stateData) {
208	    log.warn("[oauth/callback] invalid or expired state", {
209	      provider: providerName,
210	      state: state ? `${state.slice(0, 8)}...` : "empty",
211	    });
212	    return {
213	      kind: "error",
214	      error: "invalid_or_expired_state",
215	      status: 400,
216	    };
217	  }
218	
219	  if (stateData.provider !== providerName) {
220	    log.warn("[oauth/callback] state provider mismatch", {
221	      provider: providerName,
222	      stateProvider: stateData.provider,
223	    });
224	    return {
225	      kind: "error",
226	      error: "invalid_or_expired_state",
227	      status: 400,
228	    };
229	  }
230	
231	  const orgId = stateData.orgId ?? "";
232	  const connectedBy = stateData.connectedBy ?? "unknown";
233	
234	  try {
235	    const auth = providerDef.auth;
236	    if (auth.kind !== "oauth" && auth.kind !== "app-token") {
237	      log.warn("[oauth/callback] provider does not support OAuth callback", {
238	        provider: providerName,
239	        authKind: auth.kind,
240	      });
241	      return {
242	        kind: "error",
243	        error: "provider_does_not_support_oauth",
244	        status: 400,
245	      };
246	    }
247	
248	    // Pure provider logic — no DB, no framework coupling.
249	    // Both OAuthDef and AppTokenDef have processCallback — TypeScript narrows here.
250	    // SAFETY: config is providerConfigs[providerName], created by the same provider's
251	    // createConfig(). Runtime type matches TConfig; the service cannot know TConfig
252	    // statically because it serves all providers from a single Record<string, unknown>.
253	    const result = await auth.processCallback(config as never, query);
254	
255	    // Handle pending-setup: provider needs additional configuration (e.g., GitHub App request flow).
256	    // No installation to upsert — just store the setup action and redirect.
257	    if (result.status === "pending-setup") {
258	      await storeOAuthResult(state, {
259	        status: "completed",
260	        provider: providerName,
261	        setupAction: result.setupAction,
262	      });
263	
264	      log.info("[oauth/callback] pending setup, redirecting", {
265	        provider: providerName,
266	        setupAction: result.setupAction,
267	      });
268	      return buildRedirectForCompletion(stateData, providerName, {
269	        setupAction: result.setupAction,
270	      });
271	    }
272	
273	    // For all connected statuses: detect reactivation and upsert installation
274	    const existingRows = await db
275	      .select({ id: gatewayInstallations.id })
276	      .from(gatewayInstallations)
277	      .where(
278	        and(
279	          eq(gatewayInstallations.provider, providerName),
280	          eq(gatewayInstallations.externalId, result.externalId)
281	        )
282	      )
283	      .limit(1);
284	
285	    const reactivated = existingRows.length > 0;
286	
287	    // Parse through schema to validate and narrow to ProviderAccountInfo
288	    // (processCallback returns BaseProviderAccountInfo at the type level when
289	    // called through the union — schema parse gives us the concrete discriminated type)
290	    const accountInfo = providerAccountInfoSchema.parse(result.accountInfo);
291	
292	    // Upsert installation — idempotent on (provider, externalId)
293	    const rows = await db
294	      .insert(gatewayInstallations)
295	      .values({
296	        provider: providerName,
297	        externalId: result.externalId,
298	        connectedBy,
299	        orgId,
300	        status: "active",
301	        providerAccountInfo: accountInfo,
302	      })
303	      .onConflictDoUpdate({
304	        target: [
305	          gatewayInstallations.provider,
306	          gatewayInstallations.externalId,
307	        ],
308	        set: {
309	          status: "active",
310	          connectedBy,
311	          orgId,
312	          providerAccountInfo: accountInfo,
313	          updatedAt: new Date().toISOString(),
314	        },
315	      })
316	      .returning({ id: gatewayInstallations.id });
317	
318	    const installation = rows[0];
319	    if (!installation) {
320	      throw new Error("upsert_failed");
321	    }
322	
323	    // Persist OAuth tokens for statuses that include them
324	    if (
325	      result.status === "connected" ||
326	      result.status === "connected-redirect"
327	    ) {
328	      await writeTokenRecord(installation.id, result.tokens);
329	    }
330	
331	    // Store completion result in Redis for CLI polling (5-min TTL)
332	    await storeOAuthResult(state, {
333	      status: "completed",
334	      provider: providerName,
335	      ...(reactivated ? { reactivated: "true" } : {}),
336	    });
337	
338	    // Provider-specific redirect (e.g. Vercel "next" URL to complete installation lifecycle)
339	    if (result.status === "connected-redirect") {
340	      return { kind: "redirect", url: result.nextUrl };
341	    }
342	
343	    log.info("[oauth/callback] connected", {
344	      provider: providerName,
345	      reactivated,
346	    });
347	    return buildRedirectForCompletion(stateData, providerName, {
348	      reactivated,
349	    });
350	  } catch (err) {
351	    const message = err instanceof Error ? err.message : "unknown";
352	    log.error("[oauth/callback] oauth callback failed", {
353	      provider: providerName,
354	      error: message,
355	    });
356	
357	    // Store error result for CLI polling
358	    await storeOAuthResult(state, {
359	      status: "failed",
360	      error: message,
361	    });
362	
363	    return buildRedirectForError(stateData, providerName, message);
364	  }
365	}
366	
```

```
1	/**
2	 * Lazy-initialized provider configs — built on first access from validated env vars.
3	 *
4	 * Ported from apps/gateway/src/routes/connections.ts (lines 44–64).
5	 * Uses the platform app's base URL for OAuth callbacks instead of the gateway.
6	 *
7	 * IMPORTANT: Lazy initialization prevents build-time crashes when env vars
8	 * are not yet available during `next build` static analysis.
9	 */
10	import type { RuntimeConfig } from "@repo/app-providers";
11	import { PROVIDERS } from "@repo/app-providers";
12	import { appUrl } from "./related-projects";
13	
14	let _providerConfigs: Record<string, unknown> | null = null;
15	
16	/**
17	 * Configs keyed by provider name — built on first access.
18	 *
19	 * Optional providers return null from createConfig when their env vars are absent —
20	 * they are excluded and will return "unknown_provider" on any request.
21	 */
22	export function getProviderConfigs(): Record<string, unknown> {
23	  if (!_providerConfigs) {
24	    const runtime: RuntimeConfig = { callbackBaseUrl: appUrl };
25	    _providerConfigs = Object.fromEntries(
26	      Object.entries(PROVIDERS)
27	        .map(([name, p]) => [name, p.createConfig(p.env, runtime)] as const)
28	        .filter(([, config]) => config !== null)
29	    );
30	  }
31	  return _providerConfigs;
32	}
33	
34	/**
35	 * @deprecated Use getProviderConfigs() instead. This is a compatibility alias
36	 * that lazily initializes on first property access.
37	 */
38	export const providerConfigs: Record<string, unknown> = new Proxy(
39	  {} as Record<string, unknown>,
40	  {
41	    get(_target, prop) {
42	      return getProviderConfigs()[prop as string];
43	    },
44	    has(_target, prop) {
45	      return prop in getProviderConfigs();
46	    },
47	    ownKeys() {
48	      return Object.keys(getProviderConfigs());
49	    },
50	    getOwnPropertyDescriptor(_target, prop) {
51	      const configs = getProviderConfigs();
52	      if (prop in configs) {
53	        return {
54	          configurable: true,
55	          enumerable: true,
56	          value: configs[prop as string],
57	        };
58	      }
59	      return undefined;
60	    },
61	  }
62	);
63	
```
