```
0	# Internal tRPC Caller Setup
1	
2	## Overview
3	
4	Set up the internal tRPC infrastructure that enables Inngest functions and `apps/platform` route handlers to call platform capabilities through a typed, middleware-wrapped caller — without JWT overhead. This is the foundation layer; no business logic migration happens here.
5	
6	## Current State Analysis
7	
8	`api/platform/src/trpc.ts` defines three procedure tiers (`publicProcedure`, `serviceProcedure`, `adminProcedure`), all using the same observability middleware. Cross-service callers in `packages/platform-trpc/` sign a JWT, construct Headers, and call `platformRouter.createCaller(ctx)` — the full auth ceremony for every call.
9	
10	Inngest functions in `api/platform/src/inngest/functions/` make ~67 async calls (DB, provider APIs, Redis, Inngest dispatch) directly, with no middleware wrapping. Route handlers in `apps/platform/src/app/api/` import lib functions directly from `@api/platform`.
11	
12	### Key Discoveries:
13	
14	- `PlatformAuthContext` already has `{ type: "inngest" }` and `{ type: "cron" }` variants defined but never populated — the auth model anticipated internal callers (`trpc.ts:22-27`)
15	- `observabilityMiddleware` is instantiated once at `trpc.ts:104` and reused by all procedure tiers — adding a new tier requires zero middleware changes
16	- `createCaller(ctx)` accepts a resolved context object synchronously — no async needed for internal calls (`packages/platform-trpc/src/caller.ts:28`)
17	- The `extractAuth` function at `trpc.ts:107-109` only extracts fields for `service` type — needs extension for `internal`
18	
19	## Desired End State
20	
21	A `createInternalCaller()` function that returns a fully-typed tRPC caller bound to an `internalRouter`. The caller:
22	- Applies the existing observability middleware (structured logging, Sentry tracing, request journaling) to every procedure call
23	- Requires zero JWT signing, header construction, or async context creation
24	- Is importable from `@api/platform/internal` by both Inngest functions and route handlers
25	- Is NOT exposed via HTTP — only accessible through in-process `createCaller()`
26	
27	Verification: `pnpm build:platform && pnpm --filter @api/platform typecheck` passes.
28	
29	## What We're NOT Doing
30	
31	- Migrating any business logic into internal procedures (separate follow-up plan)
32	- Changing existing `serviceProcedure` / `platformRouter` / `adminRouter`
33	- Adding procedures for DB, provider API, or OAuth operations
34	- Modifying Inngest functions or route handlers to use the caller
35	- Creating Zod schemas for domain operations
36	
37	## Implementation Approach
38	
39	Add a fourth procedure tier (`internalProcedure`) alongside the existing three, create an empty `internalRouter` with a single health-ping proof procedure, wire up a synchronous caller factory, and export it.
40	
41	## Phase 1: Add `internalProcedure`, Internal Router, and Caller
42	
43	### Overview
44	
45	Extend the tRPC instance with a new procedure tier for trusted in-process callers, create the internal router with a proof-of-concept procedure, and wire up a synchronous caller factory. This is one atomic unit — `internalProcedure` is inert without `internalRouter` and vice versa.
46	
47	### Changes Required:
48	
49	#### 1. Define Explicit `PlatformContext` Type
50	
51	**File**: `api/platform/src/trpc.ts`
52	**Changes**: Add an explicit context type and use it for tRPC initialization.
53	
54	**Why this is needed**: `initTRPC.context<typeof createPlatformTRPCContext>()` infers the context type from the function's actual return paths — which only produce `"service"` or `"unauthenticated"`. TypeScript correctly rejects `"internal"` as impossible under this inferred type. Switching to an explicit type widens the context to match the full declared union. (Confirmed via spike — the plan fails to typecheck without this change.)
55	
56	Add after `PlatformAuthContext` (after line 27):
57	
58	```ts
59	/** Explicit context type for tRPC initialization.
60	 *
61	 * Must be used instead of `typeof createPlatformTRPCContext` because the
62	 * context factory only returns "service" | "unauthenticated" — but in-process
63	 * callers (createInternalCaller) provide other auth variants directly.
64	 *
65	 * If you add fields to createPlatformTRPCContext's return type, add them here too.
66	 */
67	export type PlatformContext = {
68	  auth: PlatformAuthContext;
69	  headers: Headers;
70	};
71	```
72	
73	Then update `createPlatformTRPCContext` with an explicit return type:
74	
75	```ts
76	export const createPlatformTRPCContext = async (opts: { headers: Headers }): Promise<PlatformContext> => {
77	```
78	
79	And update the tRPC initialization (line 85):
80	
81	```ts
82	const t = initTRPC.context<PlatformContext>().create({
83	```
84	
85	#### 2. Extend `PlatformAuthContext`
86	
87	**File**: `api/platform/src/trpc.ts`
88	**Changes**: Add `internal` variant to the auth discriminated union.
89	
90	```ts
91	export type PlatformAuthContext =
92	  | { type: "service"; caller: string }
93	  | { type: "webhook"; provider: string }
94	  | { type: "internal"; source: string }
95	  | { type: "inngest" }
96	  | { type: "cron" }
97	  | { type: "unauthenticated" };
98	```
99	
100	#### 3. Update `extractAuth` for Observability
101	
102	**File**: `api/platform/src/trpc.ts`
103	**Changes**: Extend the `extractAuth` callback so internal calls appear in structured logs and Sentry spans.
104	
105	Current (line 107-109):
106	```ts
107	extractAuth: (ctx) => ({
108	  ...(ctx.auth.type === "service" && { caller: ctx.auth.caller }),
109	}),
110	```
111	
112	New:
113	```ts
114	extractAuth: (ctx) => ({
115	  ...(ctx.auth.type === "service" && { caller: ctx.auth.caller }),
116	  ...(ctx.auth.type === "internal" && { source: ctx.auth.source }),
117	}),
118	```
119	
120	#### 4. Add `internalProcedure` Export
121	
122	**File**: `api/platform/src/trpc.ts`
123	**Changes**: Add after `adminProcedure` (after line 179).
124	
125	```ts
126	/**
127	 * Internal procedure -- trusted in-process callers only.
128	 * Used by Inngest functions and platform route handlers via createInternalCaller().
129	 *
130	 * NOT exposed over HTTP. Applies observability middleware without auth checks.
131	 */
132	export const internalProcedure = t.procedure.use(observabilityMiddleware);
133	```
134	
135	Note: This is identical to `publicProcedure` in implementation but distinct in intent. `publicProcedure` is for HTTP endpoints that don't require auth (health checks). `internalProcedure` is for in-process callers that bypass HTTP entirely. Keeping them separate allows diverging middleware later (e.g., adding internal-only tracing tags) without affecting public endpoints.
136	
137	#### 5. Create Internal Router
138	
139	**File**: `api/platform/src/internal.ts` (new)
140	
141	```ts
142	/**
143	 * Internal platform router — in-process callers only.
144	 *
145	 * NOT served over HTTP. Accessed exclusively via createInternalCaller().
146	 * All procedures use internalProcedure (observability middleware, no auth).
147	 *
148	 * Sub-routers will be added as business logic is migrated from:
149	 * - Inngest function steps (DB calls, provider APIs, token management)
150	 * - Route handler lib calls (OAuth, webhook ingestion)
151	 */
152	import { createTRPCRouter, internalProcedure } from "./trpc";
153	
154	// -- Internal Router ----------------------------------------------------------
155	
156	export const internalRouter = createTRPCRouter({
157	  /**
158	   * Proof-of-concept procedure.
159	   * Validates the full chain: caller -> router -> procedure -> middleware -> response.
160	   * Remove once real sub-routers are added.
161	   */
162	  ping: internalProcedure.query(({ ctx }) => ({
163	    ok: true as const,
164	    timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
165	    source: ctx.auth.type === "internal" ? ctx.auth.source : "unknown",
166	  })),
167	});
168	
169	export type InternalRouter = typeof internalRouter;
170	
171	// -- Internal Caller ----------------------------------------------------------
172	
173	/**
174	 * Create a typed caller for the internal router.
175	 *
176	 * No JWT, no headers, no async — just a direct in-process call
177	 * with full observability middleware on every procedure.
178	 *
179	 * Note: This bypasses createPlatformTRPCContext entirely — context is built
180	 * inline. If you add fields to createPlatformTRPCContext's return type,
181	 * update PlatformContext and this function accordingly.
182	 *
183	 * Usage in Inngest functions:
184	 *   const platform = createInternalCaller();
185	 *   await step.run("some-step", () => platform.someRouter.someProc(input));
186	 *
187	 * Usage in route handlers:
188	 *   const platform = createInternalCaller();
189	 *   const result = await platform.someRouter.someProc(input);
190	 */
191	export function createInternalCaller(source = "unknown") {
192	  return internalRouter.createCaller({
193	    auth: { type: "internal" as const, source },
194	    headers: new Headers(),
195	  });
196	}
197	```
198	
199	#### 6. Add Package Export Path
200	
201	**File**: `api/platform/package.json`
202	**Changes**: Add `./internal` export alongside existing paths.
203	
204	```json
205	"./internal": {
206	  "types": "./src/internal.ts",
207	  "default": "./src/internal.ts"
208	}
209	```
210	
211	#### 7. Re-export Types from Index
212	
213	**File**: `api/platform/src/index.ts`
214	**Changes**: Add type export for consumers that need the router type.
215	
216	```ts
217	export type { InternalRouter } from "./internal";
218	```
219	
220	No runtime exports from index — consumers import `createInternalCaller` from `@api/platform/internal` directly. This keeps the internal router out of the main bundle path.
221	
222	### Success Criteria:
223	
224	#### Automated Verification:
225	
226	- [x] Type-check passes: `pnpm --filter @api/platform typecheck`
227	- [x] Platform build passes: `pnpm build:platform`
228	- [x] Downstream type-check passes: `pnpm --filter @repo/platform-trpc typecheck`
229	
230	#### Manual Verification:
231	
232	- [ ] `internalRouter` is NOT referenced in any `serve()` call or HTTP handler
233	- [ ] `createInternalCaller("test").ping()` resolves to `{ ok: true, timestamp: string, source: "test" }`
234	
235	**Implementation Note**: After completing this phase and all automated verification passes, pause here for manual confirmation from the human that the manual testing was successful before proceeding.
236	
237	---
238	
239	## Phase 2: Convenience Caller Exports
240	
241	### Overview
242	
243	Create pre-configured internal callers for Inngest functions and route handlers so consumers don't repeat `createInternalCaller("inngest")` / `createInternalCaller("route")` at every call site.
244	
245	### Changes Required:
246	
247	#### 1. Create Inngest-Specific Caller
248	
249	**File**: `api/platform/src/inngest/platform.ts` (new)
250	
251	```ts
252	/**
253	 * Pre-configured internal tRPC caller for Inngest functions.
254	 *
255	 * Import this in any Inngest function to call platform procedures:
256	 *
257	 *   import { platform } from "../platform";
258	 *   await step.run("get-token", () => platform.tokens.resolve(id));
259	 *
260	 * Module-level instantiation is safe because:
261	 * - createInternalCaller() is synchronous (no JWT, no async context)
262	 * - The caller is stateless — each procedure call creates its own middleware chain
263	 * - The auth context ({ type: "internal", source: "inngest" }) is static
264	 */
265	import { createInternalCaller } from "../internal";
266	
267	export const platform = createInternalCaller("inngest");
268	```
269	
270	#### 2. Create Route Handler Caller
271	
272	**File**: `apps/platform/src/lib/internal-caller.ts` (new)
273	
274	```ts
275	/**
276	 * Pre-configured internal tRPC caller for platform route handlers.
277	 *
278	 * Usage in route handlers:
279	 *   import { platform } from "@/lib/internal-caller";
280	 *   const result = await platform.webhooks.persistAndDispatch(input);
281	 */
282	import { createInternalCaller } from "@api/platform/internal";
283	
284	export const platform = createInternalCaller("route");
285	```
286	
287	### Success Criteria:
288	
289	#### Automated Verification:
290	
291	- [x] Type-check passes: `pnpm --filter @api/platform typecheck`
292	- [x] Platform app type-check passes: `pnpm --filter lightfast-platform typecheck`
293	- [ ] Full check: `pnpm check`
294

297	
298	## Testing Strategy
299	
300	### Automated Tests:
301	
302	This is infrastructure-only — no business logic to unit test. Verification is that the type system and build pipeline accept the new exports.
303	
304	### Manual Testing Steps:
305	
306	1. Import `createInternalCaller` in a scratch file, call `.ping()`, confirm the observability middleware fires (check log output for `[trpc] ok` with `source: "test"`)
307	2. Verify the internal router is not accessible via HTTP by confirming no `serve()` or `fetchRequestHandler` references it
308	
309	## Performance Considerations
310	
311	- `createInternalCaller()` is synchronous — no JWT signing (saves ~1-2ms per call vs `createPlatformCaller()`)
312	- The caller itself is cheap to instantiate (just `router.createCaller(ctx)`)
313	- The observability middleware adds ~0ms in production (dev mode adds 100-500ms artificial latency, controlled by `opts.isDev`)
314	- Module-level instantiation (`export const platform = createInternalCaller("inngest")`) means zero per-call overhead for caller creation
315	
316	## Architecture Notes
317	
318	```
319	Existing (unchanged):
320	  api/app ──JWT──> platformRouter (serviceProcedure)
321	  apps/app ──JWT──> platformRouter (serviceProcedure)
322	
323	New:
324	  Inngest functions ──direct──> internalRouter (internalProcedure)
325	  apps/platform routes ──direct──> internalRouter (internalProcedure)
326	```
327	
328	The two router trees (`platformRouter` and `internalRouter`) are independent. Shared procedure implementations can be composed into both routers in later phases via shared handler functions, but that's out of scope here.
329	
330	## References
331	
332	- Research: `thoughts/shared/research/2026-04-05-parseerror-full-propagation-inventory.md` — identified 67 async call sites in Inngest functions
333	- Existing tRPC setup: `api/platform/src/trpc.ts` — procedure tiers and middleware
334	- Existing caller: `packages/platform-trpc/src/caller.ts` — JWT-based service caller pattern
335	- Observability middleware: `vendor/observability/src/trpc.ts` — full middleware implementation
336	
337	---
338	
339	## Improvement Log
340	
341	### Review: 2026-04-06
342	
343	**Spike verdict: PARTIAL** — The original plan's `createInternalCaller` failed to typecheck. Root cause: `initTRPC.context<typeof createPlatformTRPCContext>()` infers context type from the factory's actual return paths (`"service" | "unauthenticated"` only), rejecting `"internal"` as impossible.
344	
345	#### Changes made:
346	
347	1. **[Critical] Added `PlatformContext` type and context initialization fix** (Phase 1, Step 1) — Without this, the entire plan fails to typecheck. The fix introduces an explicit `PlatformContext` type and switches `initTRPC.context<>()` from inferred to explicit. Also adds return type annotation on `createPlatformTRPCContext`. Discovered via spike-validator in isolated worktree.
348	
349	2. **Collapsed Phase 1 + Phase 2 into single Phase 1** — `internalProcedure` without `internalRouter` is inert. One atomic unit, one verification pass.
350	
351	3. **Renumbered Phase 3 → Phase 2** — Follows from the collapse above.
352	
353	4. **Improved `ping` to return `source` from context** — Original ping ignored context, making it impossible to verify auth propagation without checking logs. Now returns `source` field from `ctx.auth` for direct manual verification.
354	
355	5. **Added context bypass documentation** — `createInternalCaller` builds context inline, bypassing `createPlatformTRPCContext`. Added comments in both `internal.ts` and `PlatformContext` type to flag this coupling for future maintainers.
356	
357	6. **Removed "(Optional)" from route handler caller** — User confirmed it should ship with this plan.
358	
359	#### Spike finding (empty headers):
360	The observability middleware (`vendor/observability/src/trpc.ts`) does **not** read from `ctx.headers` — it only uses `opts.extractAuth(ctx)` for auth fields and Sentry APIs for trace context. Empty `new Headers()` is completely safe. No degradation.
361	
```

1	/**
2	 * Connections tRPC sub-router.
3	 *
4	 * All procedures use serviceProcedure (JWT auth required).
5	 * orgId comes as an input parameter (no tenant middleware in tRPC).
6	 *
7	 * `disconnect` fires the "platform/connection.lifecycle" Inngest event
8	 * to trigger the connection teardown workflow.
9	 */
10	import { db } from "@db/app/client";
11	import {
12	  gatewayBackfillRuns,
13	  gatewayInstallations,
14	  gatewayLifecycleLogs,
15	} from "@db/app/schema";
16	import type { ProviderDefinition } from "@repo/app-providers";
17	import { getProvider, sourceTypeSchema } from "@repo/app-providers";
18	import {
19	  BACKFILL_TERMINAL_STATUSES,
20	  backfillRunRecord,
21	} from "@repo/app-providers/contracts";
22	import type { TRPCRouterRecord } from "@trpc/server";
23	import { TRPCError } from "@trpc/server";
24	import { and, eq } from "@vendor/db";
25	import { log } from "@vendor/observability/log/next";
26	import { z } from "zod";
27	import { inngest } from "../../inngest/client";
28	import { buildAuthorizeUrl } from "../../lib/oauth/authorize";
29	import { providerConfigs } from "../../lib/provider-configs";
30	import { getActiveTokenForInstallation } from "../../lib/token-helpers";
31	import { serviceProcedure } from "../../trpc";
32	
33	// ── Connections Router ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
34	
35	export const connectionsRouter = {
36	  /**
37	   * List installations, optionally filtered by status.
38	   * Source: GET /connections
39	   */
40	  list: serviceProcedure
41	    .input(
42	      z.object({
43	        orgId: z.string(),
44	        status: z.string().optional(),
45	      })
46	    )
47	    .query(async ({ input }) => {
48	      const conditions = [eq(gatewayInstallations.orgId, input.orgId)];
49	      if (input.status) {
50	        conditions.push(eq(gatewayInstallations.status, input.status));
51	      }
52	
53	      const rows = await db
54	        .select({
55	          id: gatewayInstallations.id,
56	          provider: gatewayInstallations.provider,
57	          externalId: gatewayInstallations.externalId,
58	          orgId: gatewayInstallations.orgId,
59	          status: gatewayInstallations.status,
60	        })
61	        .from(gatewayInstallations)
62	        .where(and(...conditions));
63	
64	      return rows;
65	    }),
66	
67	  /**
68	   * Token vault -- returns decrypted provider token for a connection.
69	   * Source: GET /connections/:id/token
70	   */
71	  getToken=[REDACTED]
72	    .input(
73	      z.object({
74	        id: z.string(),
75	      })
76	    )
77	    .query(async ({ input }) => {
78	      const installationRows = await db
79	        .select()
80	        .from(gatewayInstallations)
81	        .where(eq(gatewayInstallations.id, input.id))
82	        .limit(1);
83	
84	      const installation = installationRows[0];
85	
86	      if (!installation) {
87	        throw new TRPCError({
88	          code: "NOT_FOUND",
89	          message: "Connection not found",
90	        });
91	      }
92	
93	      if (installation.status !== "active") {
94	        throw new TRPCError({
95	          code: "BAD_REQUEST",
96	          message: `Installation not active (status: ${installation.status})`,
97	        });
98	      }
99	
100	      const providerName = installation.provider;
101	      const config = providerConfigs[providerName];
102	
103	      try {
104	        const providerDef = getProvider(providerName);
105	
106	        // SAFETY: getProvider() returns the full generic ProviderDefinition<TConfig, ...>
107	        // but the helper takes the base ProviderDefinition. The generic parameters are
108	        // erased at runtime -- the cast is safe because the concrete type is a supertype.
109	        const { token, expiresAt } = await getActiveTokenForInstallation(
110	          installation,
111	          config,
112	          providerDef as ProviderDefinition
113	        );
114	
115	        return {
116	          accessToken: token,
117	          provider: providerName,
118	          expiresIn: expiresAt
119	            ? Math.floor((new Date(expiresAt).getTime() - Date.now()) / 1000)
120	            : 3600, // GitHub installation tokens expire in 1 hour
121	        };
122	      } catch (err) {
123	        const message = err instanceof Error ? err.message : "unknown";
124	        log.warn("[connections] token retrieval failed", {
125	          installationId: input.id,
126	          provider: providerName,
127	          error: message,
128	        });
129	
130	        if (message === "no_token_found") {
131	          throw new TRPCError({
132	            code: "NOT_FOUND",
133	            message: "no_token_found",
134	          });
135	        }
136	        if (
137	          message === "token_expired" ||
138	          message === "token_expired:no_refresh_token"
139	        ) {
140	          throw new TRPCError({
141	            code: "BAD_REQUEST",
142	            message,
143	          });
144	        }
145	        throw new TRPCError({
146	          code: "INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR",
147	          message: `token_generation_failed: ${message}`,
148	        });
149	      }
150	    }),
151	
152	  /**
153	   * Disconnect (teardown) a connection.
154	   *
155	   * Fires "platform/connection.lifecycle" Inngest event to initiate teardown.
156	   */
157	  disconnect: serviceProcedure
158	    .input(
159	      z.object({
160	        id: z.string(),
161	        provider: sourceTypeSchema,
162	      })
163	    )
164	    .mutation(async ({ input }) => {
165	      const installationRows = await db
166	        .select()
167	        .from(gatewayInstallations)
168	        .where(
169	          and(
170	            eq(gatewayInstallations.id, input.id),
171	            eq(gatewayInstallations.provider, input.provider)
172	          )
173	        )
174	        .limit(1);
175	
176	      const installation = installationRows[0];
177	
178	      if (!installation) {
179	        throw new TRPCError({
180	          code: "NOT_FOUND",
181	          message: "Connection not found",
182	        });
183	      }
184	
185	      // Audit log: capture user-initiated disconnect intent before event fires
186	      await db.insert(gatewayLifecycleLogs).values({
187	        installationId: input.id,
188	        event: "user_disconnect",
189	        fromStatus: installation.status,
190	        toStatus: "revoked",
191	        reason: "User-initiated disconnect via platform service",
192	        metadata: {
193	          source: "platform_disconnect_handler",
194	          triggeredBy: "user",
195	        },
196	      });
197	
198	      // Fire Inngest event to trigger connection teardown workflow
199	      await inngest.send({
200	        name: "platform/connection.lifecycle",
201	        data: {
202	          reason: "user_disconnect",
203	          installationId: input.id,
204	          orgId: installation.orgId,
205	          provider: input.provider,
206	          triggeredBy: "user" as const,
207	        },
208	      });
209	
210	      return { status: "teardown_initiated", installationId: input.id };
211	    }),
212	
213	  /**
214	   * Build OAuth authorize URL for a provider.
215	   * Source: GET /connections/:provider/authorize
216	   */
217	  getAuthorizeUrl: serviceProcedure
218	    .input(
219	      z.object({
220	        provider: sourceTypeSchema,
221	        orgId: z.string(),
222	        connectedBy: z.string(),
223	        redirectTo: z.string().optional(),
224	      })
225	    )
226	    .query(async ({ input }) => {
227	      const result = await buildAuthorizeUrl({
228	        provider: input.provider,
229	        orgId: input.orgId,
230	        connectedBy: input.connectedBy,
231	        redirectTo: input.redirectTo,
232	      });
233	
234	      if (!result.ok) {
235	        throw new TRPCError({
236	          code: "BAD_REQUEST",
237	          message: result.error,
238	        });
239	      }
240	
241	      return { url: result.url, state: result.state };
242	    }),
243	
244	  /**
245	   * List backfill runs for a connection.
246	   * Source: GET /connections/:id/backfill-runs
247	   */
248	  listBackfillRuns: serviceProcedure
249	    .input(
250	      z.object({
251	        installationId: z.string(),
252	        status: z.string().optional(),
253	      })
254	    )
255	    .query(async ({ input }) => {
256	      const conditions = [
257	        eq(gatewayBackfillRuns.installationId, input.installationId),
258	      ];
259	      if (input.status) {
260	        conditions.push(eq(gatewayBackfillRuns.status, input.status));
261	      }
262	
263	      const runs = await db
264	        .select({
265	          entityType: gatewayBackfillRuns.entityType,
266	          providerResourceId: gatewayBackfillRuns.providerResourceId,
267	          since: gatewayBackfillRuns.since,
268	          depth: gatewayBackfillRuns.depth,
269	          status: gatewayBackfillRuns.status,
270	          pagesProcessed: gatewayBackfillRuns.pagesProcessed,
271	          eventsProduced: gatewayBackfillRuns.eventsProduced,
272	          eventsDispatched: gatewayBackfillRuns.eventsDispatched,
273	          completedAt: gatewayBackfillRuns.completedAt,
274	        })
275	        .from(gatewayBackfillRuns)
276	        .where(and(...conditions));
277	
278	      return runs;
279	    }),
280	
281	  /**
282	   * Upsert a backfill run record.
283	   * Source: POST /connections/:id/backfill-runs
284	   */
285	  upsertBackfillRun: serviceProcedure
286	    .input(
287	      z.object({
288	        installationId: z.string(),
289	        run: backfillRunRecord,
290	      })
291	    )
292	    .mutation(async ({ input }) => {
293	      const now = new Date().toISOString();
294	      const data = input.run;
295	      const isTerminal = (
296	        BACKFILL_TERMINAL_STATUSES as readonly string[]
297	      ).includes(data.status);
298	
299	      const sharedFields = {
300	        since: data.since,
301	        depth: data.depth,
302	        status: data.status,
303	        pagesProcessed: data.pagesProcessed,
304	        eventsProduced: data.eventsProduced,
305	        eventsDispatched: data.eventsDispatched,
306	        error: data.error ?? null,
307	        completedAt: isTerminal ? now : null,
308	        updatedAt: now,
309	      };
310	
311	      await db
312	        .insert(gatewayBackfillRuns)
313	        .values({
314	          installationId: input.installationId,
315	          entityType: data.entityType,
316	          providerResourceId: data.providerResourceId,
317	          ...sharedFields,
318	          startedAt: data.status === "running" ? now : null,
319	        })
320	        .onConflictDoUpdate({
321	          target: [
322	            gatewayBackfillRuns.installationId,
323	            gatewayBackfillRuns.providerResourceId,
324	            gatewayBackfillRuns.entityType,
325	          ],
326	          set: sharedFields,
327	        });
328	
329	      return { status: "ok" as const };
330	    }),
331	} satisfies TRPCRouterRecord;
332	
```

1	/**
2	 * Redis key convention functions for the Platform service.
3	 *
4	 * All keys are namespaced under `gw:` (legacy namespace from the former
5	 * gateway and relay services — preserved for Redis key compatibility).
6	 */
7	
8	/** Short-lived OAuth state token (TTL 600s) */
9	export const oauthStateKey = (token: string) => `gw:oauth:state:${token}`;
10	
11	/** Short-lived OAuth result for CLI polling (TTL 300s) */
12	export const oauthResultKey = (state: string) => `gw:oauth:result:${state}`;
13

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/jeevanpillay/Code/@lightfastai/lightfast/thoughts/shared/plans/2026-04-06-route-handlers-to-internal-trpc.md",
  "content": "# Route Handlers → Internal tRPC Procedures\n\n## Overview\n\nMigrate all async business logic from the 4 non-trivial `apps/platform` route handlers into internal tRPC procedures on `api/platform/src/internal.ts`. Route handlers become thin HTTP adapters that parse requests and delegate to `platform.*` procedures. This is Phase 2 of the \"tRPC as source of truth\" migration — Phase 1 (infrastructure) is complete.\n\n## Current State Analysis\n\n`apps/platform` has 4 route handlers that bypass tRPC entirely:\n\n| Route | File | What it does directly |\n|---|---|---|\n| `POST /api/ingest/[provider]` | `apps/platform/src/app/api/ingest/[provider]/route.ts` | Webhook header validation, HMAC verification, JSON parse, DB insert (`gatewayWebhookDeliveries`), `inngest.send()` |\n| `GET /api/connect/[provider]/authorize` | `apps/platform/src/app/api/connect/[provider]/authorize/route.ts` | Calls `buildAuthorizeUrl()` from `@api/platform/lib/oauth/authorize` |\n| `GET /api/connect/[provider]/callback` | `apps/platform/src/app/api/connect/[provider]/callback/route.ts` | Calls `processOAuthCallback()` from `@api/platform/lib/oauth/callback` — DB reads/writes, token exchange, Redis state management |\n| `GET /api/connect/oauth/poll` | `apps/platform/src/app/api/connect/oauth/poll/route.ts` | Calls `getOAuthResult()` from `@api/platform/lib/oauth/state` — Redis read |\n\nThe internal caller at `apps/platform/src/lib/internal-caller.ts` is wired (`createInternalCaller(\"route\")`) but unused — route handlers import lib functions directly.\n\n### Key Discoveries:\n\n- `oauth/callback.ts:351` still has a manual `err instanceof Error ? err.message : \"unknown\"` ternary — one of the 12 sites from the parseError research. Moving this into a tRPC procedure eliminates it via middleware.\n- `connections.ts:103-149` (the `getToken` serviceProcedure) already calls `getActiveTokenForInstallation` and maps errors to TRPCError codes — this is the exact pattern internal procedures should follow.\n- `buildAuthorizeUrl` already returns a typed `{ ok: true, url, state } | { ok: false, error }` result — clean for procedure wrapping.\n- `processOAuthCallback` returns `CallbackProcessResult` (redirect | inline_html | error) — the route handler maps this to HTTP responses. The procedure returns the same union; the route handler maps it to `NextResponse`.\n- The ingest route uses `@repo/app-providers` functions (`getProvider`, `hasInboundWebhooks`, `deriveVerifySignature`) for webhook verification and `@db/app/client` for DB persist. All imports already exist in `@api/platform` dependencies.\n\n## Desired End State\n\nAll 4 route handlers delegate to `platform.*` internal procedures:\n\n```\nPOST /api/ingest/[provider]     → platform.webhooks.ingest({ provider, rawBody, headers })\nGET  /connect/[provider]/authorize → platform.oauth.buildAuthorizeUrl({ provider, orgId, ... })\nGET  /connect/[provider]/callback  → platform.oauth.processCallback({ provider, state, query })\nGET  /connect/oauth/poll           → platform.oauth.pollResult({ state })\n```\n\nRoute handlers contain ONLY: request parsing, `platform.*` call, HTTP response mapping. Zero direct DB, Redis, or lib imports.\n\n### Verification:\n\n- `pnpm build:platform` passes\n- `pnpm --filter @api/platform typecheck && pnpm --filter lightfast-platform typecheck` passes\n- All 4 endpoints behave identically (same HTTP status codes, same response shapes, same HMAC verification)\n\n## What We're NOT Doing\n\n- Migrating Inngest functions to internal tRPC (Phase 3, separate plan)\n- Changing `serviceProcedure` routers (`connections`, `proxy`, `backfill`) to delegate to internal procedures (Phase 4)\n- Adding Zod input schemas for internal procedures — these are trusted in-process callers, TypeScript inference is sufficient\n- Changing webhook HMAC verification logic — just moving it\n- Modifying the tRPC HTTP handler at `/api/trpc/[trpc]`\n\n## Implementation Approach\n\nCreate 2 new sub-routers on the internal router (`webhooks`, `oauth`), move existing lib logic into procedure handlers, then slim down route handlers to HTTP adapters. Each sub-router is one self-contained step.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 1: `webhooks` Sub-Router — Ingest Endpoint\n\n### Overview\n\nMove the webhook ingestion logic (HMAC verification, DB persist, Inngest dispatch) from the route handler into `platform.webhooks.ingest()`. The route handler becomes a thin POST adapter.\n\n### Changes Required:\n\n#### 1. Create Webhooks Internal Router\n\n**File**: `api/platform/src/router/internal/webhooks.ts` (new)\n\n```ts\n/**\n * Internal webhooks sub-router.\n *\n * Handles webhook ingestion: HMAC verification, DB persistence, Inngest dispatch.\n * Moved from apps/platform/src/app/api/ingest/[provider]/route.ts.\n */\nimport { inngest } from \"../../inngest/client\";\nimport { getProviderConfigs } from \"../../lib/provider-configs\";\nimport { db } from \"@db/app/client\";\nimport { gatewayWebhookDeliveries } from \"@db/app/schema\";\nimport type { WebhookDef } from \"@repo/app-providers\";\nimport {\n  deriveVerifySignature,\n  getProvider,\n  hasInboundWebhooks,\n  isWebhookProvider,\n} from \"@repo/app-providers\";\nimport { TRPCError } from \"@trpc/server\";\nimport { log } from \"@vendor/observability/log/next\";\nimport { z } from \"zod\";\nimport { createTRPCRouter, internalProcedure } from \"../../trpc\";\n\n// ── Helpers (moved from route handler) ──────────────────────────────────────\n\nfunction getWebhookDef(\n  providerDef: NonNullable<ReturnType<typeof getProvider>>\n): WebhookDef<unknown> | null {\n  if (isWebhookProvider(providerDef)) {\n    return providerDef.webhook as WebhookDef<unknown>;\n  }\n  if (providerDef.kind === \"managed\") {\n    return providerDef.inbound.webhook as WebhookDef<unknown>;\n  }\n  if (\n    providerDef.kind === \"api\" &&\n    \"inbound\" in providerDef &&\n    providerDef.inbound\n  ) {\n    return providerDef.inbound.webhook as WebhookDef<unknown>;\n  }\n  return null;\n}\n\n// ── Router ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\nexport const webhooksInternalRouter = createTRPCRouter({\n  /**\n   * Ingest a webhook delivery: verify HMAC, persist to DB, dispatch to Inngest.\n   *\n   * The route handler extracts rawBody and headers from the HTTP request\n   * and passes them here. This procedure handles everything else.\n   *\n   * Returns the response shape for the route handler to forward as JSON.\n   */\n  ingest: internalProcedure\n    .input(\n      z.object({\n        provider: z.string(),\n        rawBody: z.string(),\n        headers: z.record(z.string()),\n        receivedAt: z.number(),\n      })\n    )\n    .mutation(async ({ input }) => {\n      const { provider: providerSlug, rawBody, headers, receivedAt } = input;\n\n      // Provider guard\n      const providerDef = getProvider(providerSlug);\n      if (!providerDef) {\n        throw new TRPCError({\n          code: \"NOT_FOUND\",\n          message: `unknown_provider: ${providerSlug}`,\n        });\n      }\n\n      if (!hasInboundWebhooks(providerDef)) {\n        throw new TRPCError({\n          code: \"BAD_REQUEST\",\n          message: `not_webhook_provider: ${providerSlug}`,\n        });\n      }\n\n      const webhookDef = getWebhookDef(providerDef);\n      if (!webhookDef) {\n        throw new TRPCError({\n          code: \"BAD_REQUEST\",\n          message: `no_webhook_def: ${providerSlug}`,\n        });\n      }\n\n      // Webhook header validation\n      const headersObj: Record<string, string | undefined> = {};\n      for (const key of Object.keys(\n        (webhookDef.headersSchema as { shape: Record<string, unknown> }).shape\n      )) {\n        headersObj[key] = headers[key] ?? undefined;\n      }\n      const headersParsed = webhookDef.headersSchema.safeParse(headersObj);\n      if (!headersParsed.success) {\n        throw new TRPCError({\n          code: \"BAD_REQUEST\",\n          message: \"missing_headers\",\n        });\n      }\n\n      // Signature verification\n      const configs = getProviderConfigs();\n      const providerConfig = configs[providerSlug];\n      if (!providerConfig) {\n        log.error(\"[webhooks.ingest] provider config not found\", {\n          provider: providerSlug,\n        });\n        throw new TRPCError({\n          code: \"INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR\",\n          message: `provider_not_configured: ${providerSlug}`,\n        });\n      }\n\n      const secret = (webhookDef.extractSecret as (config: unknown) => string)(\n        providerConfig\n      );\n      const verify =\n        webhookDef.verifySignature ??\n        deriveVerifySignature(webhookDef.signatureScheme);\n\n      // Build a Headers object for the verify function (it expects Headers, not Record)\n      const reqHeaders = new Headers(headers);\n      const isValid = await verify(rawBody, reqHeaders, secret);\n      if (!isValid) {\n        log.warn(\"[webhooks.ingest] signature verification failed\", {\n          provider: providerSlug,\n        });\n        throw new TRPCError({\n          code: \"UNAUTHORIZED\",\n          message: \"signature_invalid\",\n        });\n      }\n\n      // Payload parse + metadata extraction\n      let jsonPayload: unknown;\n      try {\n        jsonPayload = JSON.parse(rawBody);\n      } catch {\n        throw new TRPCError({\n          code: \"BAD_REQUEST\",\n          message: \"invalid_json\",\n        });\n      }\n\n      let parsedPayload: unknown;\n      try {\n        parsedPayload = webhookDef.parsePayload(jsonPayload);\n      } catch {\n        throw new TRPCError({\n          code: \"BAD_REQUEST\",\n          message: `payload_validation_failed: ${providerSlug}`,\n        });\n      }\n\n      const deliveryId = webhookDef.extractDeliveryId(reqHeaders, parsedPayload);\n      const eventType = webhookDef.extractEventType(reqHeaders, parsedPayload);\n      const resourceId = webhookDef.extractResourceId(parsedPayload);\n\n      // Persist to DB\n      await db\n        .insert(gatewayWebhookDeliveries)\n        .values({\n          provider: providerSlug,\n          deliveryId,\n          eventType,\n          installationId: null,\n          status: \"received\",\n          payload: JSON.stringify(parsedPayload),\n          receivedAt: new Date(receivedAt).toISOString(),\n        })\n        .onConflictDoNothing();\n\n      // Dispatch Inngest event\n      const correlationId = crypto.randomUUID();\n\n      await inngest.send({\n        id: `wh-${providerSlug}-${deliveryId}`,\n        name: \"platform/webhook.received\",\n        data: {\n          provider: providerSlug,\n          deliveryId,\n          eventType,\n          resourceId,\n          payload: parsedPayload,\n          receivedAt,\n          correlationId,\n        },\n      });\n\n      log.info(\"[webhooks.ingest] webhook received\", {\n        provider: providerSlug,\n        deliveryId,\n        eventType,\n        resourceId,\n        correlationId,\n      });\n\n      return { status: \"accepted\" as const, deliveryId };\n    }),\n});\n```\n\n#### 2. Mount on Internal Router\n\n**File**: `api/platform/src/internal.ts`\n\nReplace the current `ping`-only router with:\n\n```ts\n/**\n * Internal platform router — in-process callers only.\n *\n * NOT served over HTTP. Accessed exclusively via createInternalCaller().\n * All procedures use internalProcedure (observability middleware, no auth).\n */\nimport { createTRPCRouter } from \"./trpc\";\nimport { oauthInternalRouter } from \"./router/internal/oauth\";\nimport { webhooksInternalRouter } from \"./router/internal/webhooks\";\n\n// -- Internal Router ----------------------------------------------------------\n\nexport const internalRouter = createTRPCRouter({\n  webhooks: webhooksInternalRouter,\n  oauth: oauthInternalRouter,\n});\n\nexport type InternalRouter = typeof internalRouter;\n\n// -- Internal Caller ----------------------------------------------------------\n\n/**\n * Create a typed caller for the internal router.\n *\n * No JWT, no headers, no async — just a direct in-process call\n * with full observability middleware on every procedure.\n *\n * Note: This bypasses createPlatformTRPCContext entirely — context is built\n * inline. If you add fields to createPlatformTRPCContext's return type,\n * update PlatformContext and this function accordingly.\n *\n * Usage in Inngest functions:\n *   const platform = createInternalCaller();\n *   await step.run(\"some-step\", () => platform.someRouter.someProc(input));\n *\n * Usage in route handlers:\n *   const platform = createInternalCaller();\n *   const result = await platform.someRouter.someProc(input);\n */\nexport function createInternalCaller(source = \"unknown\") {\n  return internalRouter.createCaller({\n    auth: { type: \"internal\" as const, source },\n    headers: new Headers(),\n  });\n}\n```\n\nNote: The `ping` procedure is removed — it served its proof-of-concept purpose. Real sub-routers now validate the chain.\n\n#### 3. Slim Down Ingest Route Handler\n\n**File**: `apps/platform/src/app/api/ingest/[provider]/route.ts`\n\nReplace the entire file with a thin HTTP adapter:\n\n```ts\n/**\n * POST /api/ingest/:provider\n *\n * Webhook ingestion endpoint for external provider webhooks.\n * Validates HMAC signatures and dispatches to the Inngest pipeline.\n *\n * NOT tRPC — external providers send raw HTTP with HMAC signatures.\n * All business logic lives in platform.webhooks.ingest().\n */\nimport { platform } from \"@/lib/internal-caller\";\nimport { TRPCError } from \"@trpc/server\";\nimport type { NextRequest } from \"next/server\";\n\nexport const runtime = \"nodejs\";\n\nexport async function POST(\n  req: NextRequest,\n  { params }: { params: Promise<{ provider: string }> }\n) {\n  const { provider } = await params;\n  const receivedAt = Date.now();\n  const rawBody = await req.text();\n\n  // Collect headers as a plain Record for the tRPC procedure\n  const headers: Record<string, string> = {};\n  req.headers.forEach((value, key) => {\n    headers[key] = value;\n  });\n\n  try {\n    const result = await platform.webhooks.ingest({\n      provider,\n      rawBody,\n      headers,\n      receivedAt,\n    });\n\n    return Response.json(result, { status: 202 });\n  } catch (err) {\n    if (err instanceof TRPCError) {\n      const statusMap: Record<string, number> = {\n        NOT_FOUND: 404,\n        BAD_REQUEST: 400,\n        UNAUTHORIZED: 401,\n        INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR: 500,\n      };\n      const status = statusMap[err.code] ?? 500;\n      return Response.json(\n        { error: err.message },\n        { status }\n      );\n    }\n    throw err;\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### Success Criteria:\n\n#### Automated Verification:\n\n- [ ] Type-check passes: `pnpm --filter @api/platform typecheck`\n- [ ] Platform app type-check passes: `pnpm --filter lightfast-platform typecheck`\n- [ ] Platform build passes: `pnpm build:platform`\n\n#### Manual Verification:\n\n- [ ] `POST /api/ingest/github` with valid HMAC returns `202 { status: \"accepted\", deliveryId: \"...\" }`\n- [ ] `POST /api/ingest/unknown` returns `404 { error: \"unknown_provider: unknown\" }`\n- [ ] `POST /api/ingest/github` with invalid signature returns `401 { error: \"signature_invalid\" }`\n- [ ] Observability middleware fires — check logs for `[trpc] ok` with `source: \"route\"` on success and `[trpc] client error` on 4xx\n\n**Implementation Note**: After completing this phase and all automated verification passes, pause here for manual confirmation from the human that the manual testing was successful before proceeding.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 2: `oauth` Sub-Router — Authorize, Callback, Poll\n\n### Overview\n\nMove all 3 OAuth route handler lib calls into `platform.oauth.*` procedures. The callback procedure also eliminates the manual `err instanceof Error ? err.message : \"unknown\"` ternary at `callback.ts:351` — tRPC middleware handles error normalization.\n\n### Changes Required:\n\n#### 1. Create OAuth Internal Router\n\n**File**: `api/platform/src/router/internal/oauth.ts` (new)\n\n```ts\n/**\n * Internal OAuth sub-router.\n *\n * Handles OAuth authorize URL generation, callback processing, and CLI polling.\n * Moved from apps/platform/src/app/api/connect/ route handlers.\n */\nimport { z } from \"zod\";\nimport { TRPCError } from \"@trpc/server\";\nimport { createTRPCRouter, internalProcedure } from \"../../trpc\";\nimport { buildAuthorizeUrl } from \"../../lib/oauth/authorize\";\nimport {\n  type CallbackProcessResult,\n  processOAuthCallback,\n} from \"../../lib/oauth/callback\";\nimport { getOAuthResult } from \"../../lib/oauth/state\";\nimport type { SourceType } from \"@repo/app-providers\";\n\n// ── Router ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\nexport const oauthInternalRouter = createTRPCRouter({\n  /**\n   * Build OAuth authorize URL for a provider.\n   *\n   * Generates a cryptographically random state token, stores it in Redis,\n   * and returns the authorization URL.\n   */\n  buildAuthorizeUrl: internalProcedure\n    .input(\n      z.object({\n        provider: z.string(),\n        orgId: z.string(),\n        connectedBy: z.string(),\n        redirectTo: z.string().optional(),\n      })\n    )\n    .mutation(async ({ input }) => {\n      const result = await buildAuthorizeUrl({\n        provider: input.provider as SourceType,\n        orgId: input.orgId,\n        connectedBy: input.connectedBy,\n        redirectTo: input.redirectTo,\n      });\n\n      if (!result.ok) {\n        throw new TRPCError({\n          code: \"BAD_REQUEST\",\n          message: result.error,\n        });\n      }\n\n      return { url: result.url, state: result.state };\n    }),\n\n  /**\n   * Process OAuth callback: validate state, exchange code, upsert installation,\n   * persist tokens, store result for CLI polling.\n   *\n   * Returns CallbackProcessResult — the route handler maps this to HTTP responses\n   * (redirect, inline HTML, or error JSON).\n   */\n  processCallback: internalProcedure\n    .input(\n      z.object({\n        provider: z.string(),\n        state: z.string(),\n        query: z.record(z.string()),\n      })\n    )\n    .mutation(async ({ input }): Promise<CallbackProcessResult> => {\n      return processOAuthCallback({\n        provider: input.provider as SourceType,\n        state: input.state,\n        query: input.query,\n      });\n    }),\n\n  /**\n   * Poll for OAuth completion result.\n   *\n   * Returns the result hash from Redis if the OAuth flow has completed,\n   * or null if still pending.\n   */\n  pollResult: internalProcedure\n    .input(\n      z.object({\n        state: z.string(),\n      })\n    )\n > AGENT

Plan written to `thoughts/shared/plans/2026-04-06-route-handlers-to-internal-trpc.md`.

**Summary — 3 phases:**

1. **`webhooks` sub-router** — Move ingest endpoint logic (HMAC verify, DB persist, Inngest dispatch) into `platform.webhooks.ingest()`. Route handler becomes ~30 lines: parse request, call procedure, map TRPCError codes to HTTP status.

2. **`oauth` sub-router** — Move authorize/callback/poll into `platform.oauth.{buildAuthorizeUrl, processCallback, pollResult}()`. Callback route handler drops from 69 lines of lib delegation to pure response mapping. The manual `err instanceof Error ? err.message : "unknown"` at `callback.ts:351` gets eliminated by tRPC middleware.

3. **Cleanup** — Verify zero direct `@db/app`, `@api/platform/lib`, or Inngest client imports remain in route handlers (except the Inngest serve mount).

**Key design decisions baked in:**
- New files go in `api/platform/src/router/internal/` (parallel to `router/platform/`)
- Internal procedures use Zod input schemas (consistent with existing tRPC patterns, enables future HTTP exposure if needed)
- `processCallback` returns `CallbackProcessResult` as-is — the route handler still does the redirect/HTML/error response mapping since that's HTTP-specific
- The lib layer stays — procedures call lib functions, not the other way around. Lib is implementation, procedures are interface.

> DEVELOPER

qiestopm whats is the purpose of router/platform?

> AGENT

`api/platform/src/router/platform/` holds the **external-facing** `serviceProcedure` routers — the ones served over HTTP at `/api/trpc/[trpc]` and called cross-service by `apps/app` via JWT-authenticated `createPlatformCaller()`.

Three routers live there today:

| Router | File | Who calls it |
|---|---|---|
| `connections` | `router/platform/connections.ts` | `api/app/src/router/org/connections.ts` (7 sites) |
| `proxy` | `router/platform/proxy.ts` | `api/app/src/router/org/connections.ts` + `apps/app/src/lib/proxy.ts` |
| `backfill` | `router/platform/backfill.ts` | `api/app/src/router/org/connections.ts` |

They're assembled into `platformRouter` in `root.ts` and mounted on the HTTP handler.

**The distinction with the new `router/internal/`:** `router/platform/` = JWT-guarded, HTTP-served, called by other services. `router/internal/` = no auth, never HTTP-served, called in-process by Inngest functions and route handlers.

Two separate directories because two separate trust boundaries.

> DEVELOPER

what are the consumers of router/platform?

> AGENT

From the earlier research — there are exactly **9 call sites** across 2 files, all going through `createPlatformCaller()`:

**`api/app/src/router/org/connections.ts`** — 7 sites:
- Line 48: `platform.connections.getAuthorizeUrl()`
- Line 118: `platform.connections.disconnect()`
- Line 218: `platform.proxy.execute()` — GitHub App validation
- Line 316: `platform.proxy.execute()` — get-repo + get-file-contents loop
- Line 462: `platform.connections.disconnect()` — Vercel path
- Line 683: `platform.proxy.execute()` — inside `enrichInstallation`
- Line 749: `platform.proxy.execute()` — inside `listResources`

**`apps/app/src/lib/proxy.ts`** — 2 sites:
- Line 38: `platform.proxy.execute()` — per-installation in `Promise.all`
- Line 267: `platform.proxy.execute()` — single call in `proxyCallLogic`

All 9 use the JWT-signed in-process caller (no actual HTTP hop). The HTTP handler at `/api/trpc/[trpc]` exists but has no active consumers today — it's there for future cross-network calls.

> DEVELOPER

so these are basically api/app calling the platform-layer?

> AGENT

Yes, exactly. `api/app` is the app-layer tRPC API (user-facing, Clerk-authed). When it needs platform capabilities (tokens, proxy calls, connections), it calls down into `api/platform` via `createPlatformCaller()`.

```
Browser → apps/app → api/app (Clerk auth) → api/platform (JWT auth)
```

The `router/platform/` procedures are the contract between those two layers.

> DEVELOPER

im just curious should we fundamentally change how the api layer works. seems like lots of confusing layers, maybe single api folder. everything runs through that layer. would eliminate many layers of complexity. what do you think? just high-level with what youve researched so far

> AGENT

Honest take: the layers exist for real reasons, and collapsing them would create worse problems than the current indirection.

**Why two API packages exist:**

- `api/app` = user-facing, Clerk auth, org-scoped. Runs inside `apps/app` (port 4107).
- `api/platform` = service-facing, JWT auth, no user context. Runs inside `apps/platform` (port 4112). Handles secrets (OAuth tokens, encryption keys, webhook signing secrets) that should never be in the app process.

If you merge them into one API folder:
- **Token vault secrets leak into the app bundle** — `ENCRYPTION_KEY`, `SERVICE_JWT_SECRET`, provider OAuth client secrets all become available in the app process
- **Clerk auth and JWT auth have to coexist** in one tRPC context — every procedure needs to know "am I handling a user request or a service request?"
- **Deployment coupling** — a change to webhook ingestion redeploys the user-facing app and vice versa
- **Inngest function registration** doubles — one serve handler mounting both app workflows and platform workflows

**The real confusion isn't the layers — it's the call direction.** Today:

```
Browser → apps/app → api/app →(JWT)→ api/platform
                                         ↑
                     apps/platform routes─┘ (lib imports, being fixed)
                     Inngest functions────┘ (direct DB, Phase 3)
```

After the internal tRPC migration, it becomes clean:

```
Browser → apps/app → api/app →(JWT)→ api/platform (platformRouter)
                                         ↑
                     apps/platform routes─┘ (internalRouter)
                     Inngest functions────┘ (internalRouter)
```

Two routers, one package (`api/platform`), two trust boundaries. The confusion goes away not by merging packages, but by making everything inside `api/platform` flow through tRPC — which is exactly what we're doing.

> DEVELOPER

are you 100% sure of these concerns? why not just server the entirity of everything through api/platform. idk, feels like it would be cleaner. re-evaluate.

> AGENT

You're right to push. Let me re-evaluate honestly — some of my concerns don't hold up.

**Concern 1: "Secrets leak into the app bundle"** — Wrong. Secrets are env vars on the Vercel deployment, not in the package. `apps/app` simply doesn't have `ENCRYPTION_KEY` configured. The package boundary doesn't protect this — the deployment boundary does.

**Concern 2: "Clerk and JWT auth coexist"** — True but trivial. They're already separate procedure tiers. One package with `userProcedure` (Clerk), `serviceProcedure` (JWT), `internalProcedure` (none) is clean.

**Concern 3: "Deployment coupling"** — Already exists. Changing `api/platform` → rebuilds `packages/platform-trpc` → rebuilds `api/app` → rebuilds `apps/app`. The bridge doesn't decouple; it adds a hop.

**Concern 4: "Inngest registration doubles"** — Wrong. Each app mounts its own serve handler. Functions are defined in the package but registered per-deployment.

So the real answer: **a single `api/` package would be cleaner.** It would eliminate `packages/platform-trpc` entirely, kill the JWT ceremony for in-process calls, and give you one router tree.

**The reason I'd still wait**: the migration is large (merging ~30+ files, updating all imports, reconciling two env validation schemas, removing the bridge package) and orthogonal to what we're doing now. The internal tRPC work already fixes the immediate pain — Inngest and route handlers flowing through tRPC. The two-package merge is a follow-up that becomes easier *after* everything flows through routers, because then you're just moving routers between packages instead of untangling scattered lib calls.

If you want to do it, the sequence would be: finish internal tRPC migration (Phases 2-4) → merge `api/app` + `api/platform` into `api/` → delete `packages/platform-trpc`. Doing it in that order means the merge is mechanical (move routers, update imports) rather than architectural.
